# Plans 1–12 Program Closeout and Integration Closeout

**Date:** 2026-09-22
**Status:** Documentation-only closeout and dependency-ordered integration plan. No production code, game data, or test suite changes are claimed by this document.
**Scope:** Plans 1–12 in expansion_wave1, including all continuation material through Wave 34 for Plans 9–12 and the existing closeout packets for Plans 1–4 and Plans 5–8.
**Authority rule:** Current source, INTEGRATION_PLANS.md, WORKTREE_OWNERSHIP.md, TEST_POLICY.md, KNOWN_DEBT.md, live data loaders, current save owners, and current host routes outrank every plan paragraph.

This document closes the planning set as a reviewable program package. It converts twelve large proposals into one evidence-first integration order, names the minimum safe first slice for every plan, defines the shared owner and event boundaries, identifies migration and replay rules, and supplies handoff receipts. The closeout does not convert authored prose into runtime behavior. Every row remains premise-gated until a current-source audit proves the owner, consumer, route, data contract, persistence path, deterministic behavior, accessibility path, and rollback path.

The closeout deliberately treats word count as documentation volume rather than proof. A plan can contain a complete narrative bank and still lack a reachable command. A data row can validate as JSON and still have no runtime consumer. A panel can display an attractive projection and still fail because it owns a mutable counter. A save field can deserialize and still corrupt replay if its ordering or default is unstable. The only promotion signal is a bounded receipt that ties present evidence to one safe change.

## 1. Closeout definition and operating boundary

The Plans 1–12 package is closed as a planning program when all twelve plans have a named owner family, an explicit required delta, a collision audit, a first safe slice, a state ladder, a data and migration contract, a save and determinism rule, a host route, a content gate, a focused verification path, a rollback rule, and an implementation handoff. A plan is not marked integrated merely because the plan file is long, the README counts are synchronized, or a generated catalog contains an ID.

The implementation boundary is intentionally narrow. Core behavior belongs in Assets/Ashfall.Core and remains engine-free. Authored data belongs in Assets/StreamingAssets/Data and passes the current validators. Godot presentation belongs in src and remains a thin command and projection surface. Tests target the current public contracts. Save ownership stays with the existing section owner. Cross-plan facts move through existing events or typed read seams. If a required owner cannot be proven, the correct result is a blocker receipt, not a speculative cache or a parallel subsystem.

The integrator should read this document together with the existing Plans 1–4 and Plans 5–8 closeouts. Those files remain useful historical planning packets. This file is the program-level closeout and takes precedence only for the dependency order and shared acceptance gates defined here.

## 2. Program snapshot

| Band | Plans | Current role in the program | Closeout posture |
|---|---|---|---|
| Recovery and care | 1–2 | translate hazard and health facts into bounded player decisions | premise audit before any new command |
| Civic and scenario | 3–4 | connect public works and authored scenario packages to existing project and campaign owners | content package after owner proof |
| Movement and memory | 5–6 | expose route outcomes and relationship memory without duplicate ledgers | read-model first |
| Shelter and archive | 7–8 | expose reserve conditions and provenance-aware exploration evidence | state and provenance first |
| Kitchen and watch | 9–10 | make routine resilience and safe response legible through existing owners | bounded observation first |
| Workshop and learning | 11–12 | connect repair apprenticeships and civic teaching to condition, archive, consent, and roster owners | consent and condition first |

The four bands are not independent products. They share inventory, route, shelter, roster, journal, archive, relationship, weather, expedition, and accessibility facts. The program therefore uses one dependency spine: verify current owner, verify data consumer, prove Core read or command seam, prove persistence or statelessness, bind a thin route, then author only the content that the route can truthfully display.

## 3. Current-reality evidence protocol

Every closeout row starts with a source census. The auditor records the current class or system, its public methods, event producers, consumers, data files, save section, host route, and focused test. Search results are evidence leads rather than proof. A plan paragraph is marked PRESENT, PARTIAL, UNREACHABLE, RETIRED, or UNOWNED. PRESENT means the behavior is observed through a current route or test. PARTIAL means code exists but a producer, consumer, save path, or route is absent. UNREACHABLE means the data or branch can be loaded but no player command reaches it. RETIRED means the live ledger forbids revival. UNOWNED means the feature is a proposal. The receipt records the inspection date and the exact reason for every classification.

## 4. One-owner rule

The program assigns one authoritative owner to every mutable fact. Inventory owns item quantity, not a panel or quest. Health owns treatment outcome, not an outreach card. Route or expedition owns travel result, not a settlement note. Relationship owns affinity and trust, not a memory projection. Power and water owners own reserve facts, not an automation dashboard. Cartography and archive owners own provenance, not a story paragraph. A projection may cache immutable derived text only when it has an explicit invalidation rule and can be rebuilt. If two plans need the same fact, they subscribe to one owner and exchange a stable ID, source age, and event sequence.

## 5. Data authority and validation

All authored rows use snake_case IDs, schema_version, explicit lifecycle, localization keys, bounded numeric values, and references that the current validator can explain. The closeout requires duplicate-ID checks, missing-reference checks, range checks, unsupported-effect checks, and reachability checks. A valid row with no consumer remains disabled or design-only. Mutable state is not smuggled into JSON descriptions. Corrected content creates a new evidence revision or migration rule rather than rewriting old player history. High-volume banks are loaded lazily or paged and preserve stable ordering.

## 6. State and lifecycle discipline

Every first slice distinguishes offered, inspected, accepted, active, paused, completed, failed, expired, corrected, and replay states as needed. A player choice is separate from the event confirming it. Reservations have an owner, a release rule, an interruption path, and a visible receipt. Duplicate commands are idempotent or explicitly rejected. A host reopen cannot create a second event. A correction appends new evidence and preserves the prior source. State ladders are short enough to test and specific enough to explain why a player can or cannot act.

## 7. Save and migration discipline

Durable state is captured by the current save-section owner. New sections are justified only when existing state cannot carry the fact. Every change defines CaptureState, RestoreState, version, old-save defaults, invalid-save behavior, checksum implications, null semantics, and deep-copy isolation. Save during a transition restores one coherent state rather than half-applied rewards or costs. If a proposal can rebuild from existing state, it stays stateless and does not gain a convenience save field. The save-store matrix and migration tests are updated before any implementation claim.

## 8. Determinism and replay

Replayable behavior uses the existing seeded RNG contract or no randomness at all. Stable ordering uses authored ordinals, canonical IDs, or deterministic tie-breakers. Wall-clock time is display metadata, not a seed. Hash iteration order, process identity, locale, random GUIDs, and UI callback timing cannot change Core results. Paired fixtures run the same ordered inputs before and after save, reload, host reopen, duplicate delivery, interruption, and content correction. The receipt records event order and a final state hash. A differing hash is a blocker until the owner explains why the result is intentionally non-deterministic.

## 9. Host and UI boundary

Godot hosts bind input, route commands, subscribe to owner facts, and present feedback. They do not calculate resource counts, care outcomes, relationship values, work completion, threat certainty, or campaign progression. Every panel has loading, empty, stale, blocked, redacted, completed, and error states where relevant. Focus survives refresh and returns to the invoking control on close. Keyboard and controller actions have visible feedback. Text scale, contrast, non-color cues, sound-off alternatives, reduced motion, and long localization are tested. A failed optional asset never removes a core command or the explanation of its state.

## 10. Content and narrative boundary

Narrative content begins from an observable owner fact. Every side quest has entry evidence, interpretation, complication, competing interest, choice, cost, quiet outcome, costly outcome, refusal outcome, unresolved outcome, delayed callback, repeat policy, retirement condition, and correction behavior. Characters and locations use canonical IDs. Prose can describe uncertainty and memory, but cannot claim an unimplemented mechanic. A story branch that changes no reachable fact remains a fiction-only packet and is not presented as a system integration.

## 11. Performance and diagnostics

Each first slice records expected frequency, worst-case record count, allocation budget, event fan-out, first-page latency, save delta, and safe degradation. Repeated refresh and high-volume pagination are exercised. Caches have one owner and explicit invalidation. Diagnostics name source, consumer, age, and failure reason without storing private free text. Optional audio and art are lazy and cancellable. A performance failure degrades to a visible stale or empty state and does not mutate gameplay.

## 12. Package ownership and claims

Before editing, the builder claims exact paths in WORKTREE_OWNERSHIP.md and checks the current integration ledger. Documentation closeout does not grant implementation ownership. Shared seams belong to the named integrator. A handoff lists files touched, files intentionally untouched, focused commands, observed results, limitations, and rollback. If a package overlaps another active claim, the correct status is BLOCKED or DEFERRED with evidence, not a new parallel implementation.

## 13. Evidence vocabulary

Use READY FOR PREMISE AUDIT when the plan has a coherent first slice but no current-source proof. Use READY FOR CORE CONTRACT when an owner and API are verified. Use READY FOR DATA when the loader and validator are verified. Use READY FOR HOST when save and replay behavior are proven. Use READY FOR CONTENT when the route can truthfully display a sample branch. Use INTEGRATED only after end-to-end evidence exists. These labels describe the package state, not the amount of prose in the plan.

## 14. Dependency spine for Plans 1–12

The recommended order is a set of review gates rather than a mandatory single-threaded branch.

1. Verify source owners and release any path claims.
2. Select one first safe slice from the plan registry.
3. Prove a read model or command on the existing Core owner.
4. Add or validate one representative data row.
5. Prove save, migration, or explicit stateless reconstruction.
6. Pair same-seed replay across save and duplicate delivery.
7. Bind one host route with focus, close/back, error, and stale states.
8. Add one narrative or side-quest packet that consumes the proven fact.
9. Run focused Core, data, save, integration, and bounded headless checks.
10. Record rollback and handoff before expanding the content bank.

Plan dependencies follow the facts they consume. Plans 1 and 10 depend on weather, expedition, communications, and shelter observations. Plan 2 depends on health, consent, inventory, vehicle, and roster. Plan 3 depends on water, project, settlement, territory, reputation, and archive. Plan 4 depends on campaign manifest, identity, narrative, localization, and ModSupport boundaries. Plan 5 depends on route, cargo, vehicle, weather, and settlement. Plan 6 depends on relationship, journal, consent, identity, and archive. Plan 7 depends on power, water, warmth, air, building, schedule, duty, and needs. Plan 8 depends on cartography, expedition, installation, fragment, codex, and archive. Plans 9–12 depend on inventory, shelter, roster, archive, relationship, condition, and accessibility seams already named in their plans.

## Plan 1 — Wildland Firefronts and Burn Recovery — closeout dossier


**Program band:** wildland fire, burn recovery, weather cascade, disaster response, expedition, vehicle, wildlife, communications, cartography, and archive
**Primary owners:** weather cascade, shelter fire, disaster response, expedition, vehicle, wildlife, communications, cartography, and archive owners
**Required delta:** a provenance-aware recovery command that can inspect an existing hazard or weather fact, choose a bounded response, and show a later recovery consequence without inventing a second fire, ecology, route, or inventory ledger
**First safe slice:** one smoke-observation report linked to an existing weather or expedition result, with inspect, defer, share, refuse, and recovery callback outcomes
**Material anchors:** ash creek markers, a charred gate hinge, a wet seed pouch, and a radio note folded into a map
**Observable signals:** smoke age, burn confidence, route closure, seed viability, crew fatigue, and recovery callback
**Content family:** field reports, ashline witnesses, seed-bank visits, evacuee promises, return-route complications, and archive entries
**Primary risks:** duplicate hazard state, speculative fire spread, unsafe wildlife mutation, route truth drift, and unbounded event fan-out

This dossier closes the planning surface for Plan 1 without claiming runtime integration. Every subsection is a handoff requirement, not an implementation result.

### 15. Closeout objective

Close Wildland Firefronts and Burn Recovery as a premise-gated package. The objective is to turn a provenance-aware recovery command that can inspect an existing hazard or weather fact, choose a bounded response, and show a later recovery consequence without inventing a second fire, ecology, route, or inventory ledger into one reviewable vertical slice while preserving weather cascade, shelter fire, disaster response, expedition, vehicle, wildlife, communications, cartography, and archive owners. The package is successful when the first slice has a named publisher, a consumer that can be reached by a current route, a reversible command, truthful feedback, and a receipt that states what remains unowned.


### 16. Existing authority inventory

The authority inventory for Wildland Firefronts and Burn Recovery is the current set of wildland fire, burn recovery, weather cascade, disaster response, expedition, vehicle, wildlife, communications, cartography, and archive. Inspect each owner in source, data, save, host, and tests. The plan may reference ash creek markers, a charred gate hinge, a wet seed pouch, and a radio note folded into a map and smoke age, burn confidence, route closure, seed viability, crew fatigue, and recovery callback, but those observations become gameplay facts only when an existing owner emits them. Any older plan text that implies a new ledger, registry, or simulation is treated as a hypothesis until a current source path proves it.


### 17. Required delta

The smallest required delta is a provenance-aware recovery command that can inspect an existing hazard or weather fact, choose a bounded response, and show a later recovery consequence without inventing a second fire, ecology, route, or inventory ledger. The delta does not include a new framework, a new global manager, or broad refactoring. It is one command or read projection, one representative row, one observable outcome, and one recovery path. The builder records the old behavior, the missing behavior, and the exact consumer that will make the change visible.


### 18. Premise and collision receipt

The premise receipt searches for alternate names, partial producers, retired implementations, stale IDs, test-only shims, and existing UI routes. Collision review is especially important for duplicate hazard state, speculative fire spread, unsafe wildlife mutation, route truth drift, and unbounded event fan-out. A collision is resolved by extending the current owner, adapting a current provider, or marking the proposal blocked. The receipt must state what was searched and which result is current.


### 19. Single-owner assignment

Assign simulation rules to Core, authored facts to JSON, persistence to the current save owner, presentation to Godot, input to the route, and validation to existing integrity gates. For Wildland Firefronts and Burn Recovery, the primary owner families are weather cascade, shelter fire, disaster response, expedition, vehicle, wildlife, communications, cartography, and archive owners. If a value appears in more than one plan, the shared seam carries a canonical ID, source age, confidence, and event sequence instead of copied mutable state.


### 20. First safe player loop

The first safe loop for Wildland Firefronts and Burn Recovery is notice, inspect, compare, choose, observe, and recover. The proposed slice is one smoke-observation report linked to an existing weather or expedition result, with inspect, defer, share, refuse, and recovery callback outcomes. The player can verify, accept, defer, share, delegate, refuse, or return. Quiet success protects a routine, costly success spends a real resource, refusal preserves the earlier state, and unresolved play leaves a visible unknown. No timer, color, or sound cue is the sole proof of outcome.


### 21. Data contract

The representative data contract describes field reports, ashline witnesses, seed-bank visits, evacuee promises, return-route complications, and archive entries using stable snake_case IDs, schema_version, lifecycle, source, localization keys, references, bounded values, and correction notes. It distinguishes authored configuration, derived projection, and durable state. Duplicate IDs, missing references, unsupported fields, empty consumers, corrected rows, and old versions have explicit outcomes. A sample row must connect ash creek markers, a charred gate hinge, a wet seed pouch, and a radio note folded into a map to an owner fact without turning the anchor into a second authority.


### 22. State ladder

The state ladder for Wildland Firefronts and Burn Recovery covers offered, inspected, accepted, active, paused, completed, failed, expired, corrected, and replay states only where the slice needs them. Every transition names the mutator, reservation, release rule, event, receipt, and interruption behavior. A repeated command is idempotent or refused. A correction adds evidence and preserves prior history. The ladder must make smoke age, burn confidence, route closure, seed viability, crew fatigue, and recovery callback observable without storing duplicate counters.


### 23. Data flow

The data flow is INPUT → VALIDATION → CORE STATE or READ MODEL → DOMAIN EVENT → HOST PROVIDER → UI → PLAYER ACTION → COMMAND → CORE MUTATION → SAVE. For Wildland Firefronts and Burn Recovery, one smoke-observation report linked to an existing weather or expedition result, with inspect, defer, share, refuse, and recovery callback outcomes is the sample path. The flow identifies source age, confidence, stale behavior, and the delayed callback. Subscribers consume facts and request owner mutations; they never reach into foreign ledgers.


### 24. Save and migration

Capture only durable owner facts needed by Wildland Firefronts and Burn Recovery. Define CaptureState, RestoreState, version, old-save defaults, malformed-envelope refusal, checksum behavior, null and empty semantics, and deep-copy isolation. If the slice is a pure projection, say so and prove reconstruction after reload. A save during interruption must not duplicate rewards, penalties, messages, treatment, route results, archive entries, or relationship changes.


### 25. Determinism and replay

Use the seeded RNG contract only if the slice needs chance. Otherwise keep the result pure. Stable ordering for smoke age, burn confidence, route closure, seed viability, crew fatigue, and recovery callback uses canonical IDs and authored ordinals. Pair same-seed inputs through save, reload, host reopen, duplicate delivery, interruption, and source correction. No wall-clock seed, process identity, random GUID, locale, or hash iteration order may affect the result. Record event order and final state hash.


### 26. Event and system wiring

Name the publisher, consumer, freshness, consequence owner, and delayed callback for every event. The preferred order is upstream world or owner fact, canonical result, projection, narrative callback, then UI refresh. Duplicate events are ignored or folded at the owner boundary. For Wildland Firefronts and Burn Recovery, cross-system callbacks may touch wildland fire, burn recovery, weather cascade, disaster response, expedition, vehicle, wildlife, communications, cartography, and archive, but each callback must remain read-only until it asks the correct owner to mutate.


### 27. Godot host route

The host route exposes one smoke-observation report linked to an existing weather or expedition result, with inspect, defer, share, refuse, and recovery callback outcomes through the existing provider and panel lifecycle. It defines route registration, focus order, keyboard/controller bindings, refresh signal, disposal, close/back behavior, loading, empty, stale, blocked, redacted, complete, and error states. The panel does not count, reserve, score, or simulate. Optional visuals or audio degrade to text while preserving the command and a reasoned next action.


### 28. UI, UX, and accessibility

Accessibility acceptance for Wildland Firefronts and Burn Recovery includes readable text, scaling, contrast, non-color cues, reduced motion, sound-off alternatives, controller parity, focus retention, pagination, cancellation, and safe long strings. Uncertainty from smoke age, burn confidence, route closure, seed viability, crew fatigue, and recovery callback is stated in text with source age. Redaction or consent blocks explain why content is unavailable. Refresh and disposal are idempotent, and a failed provider leaves the player with a recoverable state.


### 29. Narrative and side-quest integration

The content layer uses field reports, ashline witnesses, seed-bank visits, evacuee promises, return-route complications, and archive entries as grounded evidence. The representative branch has discovery, interpretation, complication, competing interest, escalation, irreversible choice, quiet outcome, costly outcome, refusal outcome, unresolved outcome, delayed callback, repeat policy, retirement rule, and correction behavior. Characters and locations use canonical IDs. Narrative text may reveal an observed owner fact but cannot create one.


### 30. Failure-mode attack

Attack Wildland Firefronts and Burn Recovery with null state, empty catalog, missing ID, duplicate ID, out-of-range value, stale source, corrected content, zero resources, dead actor, inaccessible route, completed quest, repeated event, huge value, negative value, unexpected event order, host reload, save during transition, corrupted save, deterministic replay, and unmounted UI. For each case, state whether the owner rejects, defaults, preserves old evidence, releases a reservation, or exposes a visible blocked state.


### 31. Performance and diagnostics

Set a bounded budget for Wildland Firefronts and Burn Recovery: first-page latency, refresh allocations, event fan-out, save delta, worst-case records, and cancellation. High-volume records are paged or lazy-loaded. Caches have one owner and invalidation. Diagnostics report source, consumer, age, and reason without storing private free text. A budget failure degrades to stale or empty presentation and never mutates authoritative state.


### 32. Focused test strategy

The focused strategy includes Core happy path, boundary, failure, invariant, event, and deterministic tests; save round-trip, deep-copy, old-version migration, invalid-version rejection, and checksum cases; data schema, duplicate-ID, reference, range, and consumer reachability checks; Core-to-provider and provider-to-Godot route checks; and a bounded headless check if the runtime path is affected. The first test file runs alone before aggregation.


### 33. Phase 0 premise audit

Phase 0 verifies the current premise for Wildland Firefronts and Burn Recovery. Inspect weather cascade, shelter fire, disaster response, expedition, vehicle, wildlife, communications, cartography, and archive owners, current data files, routes, save sections, tests, and live claims. Record exact paths and stop if a required seam is missing or claimed by another package. Do not author production code, speculative data, or new tests during this phase unless the current foreman assigns them.


### 34. Phase 1 Core contract

Phase 1 adds the smallest Core command, query, DTO, or event needed for one smoke-observation report linked to an existing weather or expedition result, with inspect, defer, share, refuse, and recovery callback outcomes. It preserves engine independence and existing owner boundaries. The phase completes when invariants, duplicate handling, error results, and state transitions are covered. It must not add a new global registry, parallel ledger, or Godot dependency.


### 35. Phase 2 data and persistence

Phase 2 adds one validator-backed sample for field reports, ashline witnesses, seed-bank visits, evacuee promises, return-route complications, and archive entries, then proves migration or stateless reconstruction. Every field is classified as authored, derived, or durable. Save capture and restore are written before any claim of persistence. The phase must not widen the catalog or rename shared IDs until the sample is reachable and replay-stable.


### 36. Phase 3 host and route

Phase 3 binds the Core result to a thin host route. It preserves route registration, focus, close/back, refresh, disposal, error, stale, and empty behavior. The phase completes when a player can perform one smoke-observation report linked to an existing weather or expedition result, with inspect, defer, share, refuse, and recovery callback outcomes and see an observable owner result. It must not place domain calculations in the panel or create a local cache.


### 37. Phase 4 content and callbacks

Phase 4 adds one location or room, one named person or crew, one disturbance, and four outcome branches over the proven route. It connects to journal, archive, relationship, roster, route, shelter, or expedition facts only through current owners. It must not enable a content bank that has no runtime consumer.


### 38. File impact map

Likely areas are Assets/Ashfall.Core for owner contracts, Assets/StreamingAssets/Data for the representative row, src for provider and panel binding, and Ashfall.Core.Tests for focused contracts. Each path remains READ ONLY during premise audit and becomes MODIFY only after an exact claim is accepted. Generated outputs are changed only through their owning generator. No Unity-era path is a target.


### 39. Out of scope

Out of scope for Wildland Firefronts and Burn Recovery: a new global simulation, a second ledger, broad architecture cleanup, unrequested localization sweep, unrelated balance changes, retired Unity behavior, speculative save sections, private telemetry, and a global content browser. Nearby gaps remain visible in the blocker receipt and are not silently bundled.


### 40. Rollback and recovery

Rollback for Wildland Firefronts and Burn Recovery removes or disables the new adapter and content row while preserving old saves. A migration failure rejects the malformed envelope without partial mutation. A host failure unbinds the route while Core remains isolated. A content correction appends evidence. The smallest reversible package is one owner seam, one data row, one host route, one focused test set, and one handoff.


### 41. Definition of done

The slice is ready for implementation handoff when owner, consumer, data, state, save or stateless proof, deterministic replay, host route, accessibility, performance, content callback, focused verification, rollback, and exact file claims all have receipts. The package remains READY FOR PREMISE AUDIT until current evidence confirms the first gate. Word count and prose density never promote it.


### 42. Implementation handoff

The handoff for Wildland Firefronts and Burn Recovery names the objective, current reality, required delta, owner, data row, command, route, save impact, replay fixture, accessibility case, performance budget, content IDs, rollback, open blockers, and next bounded action. MUST PRESERVE: one owner, engine-free Core, JSON authority, deterministic behavior, current save ownership, truthful UI, and current ledger claims. MUST NOT DO: duplicate state, revive retired architecture, or claim integration from compile success alone.


### Closeout evidence packet 1 — Plan 1 Wildland Firefronts and Burn Recovery

The packet uses ash creek markers, a charred gate hinge, a wet seed pouch, and a radio note folded into a map and smoke age, burn confidence, route closure, seed viability, crew fatigue, and recovery callback as evidence labels for Wildland Firefronts and Burn Recovery. It records a source premise, owner, consumer, freshness, command or query, state transition, save expectation, replay input order, accessibility case, performance budget, content callback, rollback, and stop line. The packet is accepted only when the evidence can be reproduced from current source and data. If the consumer is absent, the packet is design-only and the missing route is written as a blocker. If the owner is ambiguous, the packet stops before implementation. If content is corrected, the old result remains auditable and the new result carries a revision marker. If a player refuses or defers, the previous owner state remains intact and the UI explains the next permitted action.

## Plan 2 — Mobile Medical Outreach — closeout dossier


**Program band:** health, disease, medical ward, inventory, vehicle, expedition, consent, relationships, journal, and roster
**Primary owners:** health and disease, ward duty, inventory, vehicle, expedition, consent, relationship, journal, and roster owners
**Required delta:** a consent-aware outreach dispatch projection over an existing care result, with clear limits between transport, referral, supply handoff, and clinical treatment
**First safe slice:** one mobile referral packet that reads a current patient or settlement fact and routes an acknowledged, deferred, refused, or unresolved handoff
**Material anchors:** a folded triage card, a sealed medicine tin, a vehicle cot strap, and a message receipt marked by hand
**Observable signals:** triage freshness, consent age, medicine custody, travel delay, symptom uncertainty, and follow-up route
**Content family:** clinic visits, supply escorts, family messages, missed appointments, privacy choices, and return-to-care callbacks
**Primary risks:** duplicated patient state, implied treatment, privacy leakage, medication double-spend, and unreachable referral branches

This dossier closes the planning surface for Plan 2 without claiming runtime integration. Every subsection is a handoff requirement, not an implementation result.

### 15. Closeout objective

Close Mobile Medical Outreach as a premise-gated package. The objective is to turn a consent-aware outreach dispatch projection over an existing care result, with clear limits between transport, referral, supply handoff, and clinical treatment into one reviewable vertical slice while preserving health and disease, ward duty, inventory, vehicle, expedition, consent, relationship, journal, and roster owners. The package is successful when the first slice has a named publisher, a consumer that can be reached by a current route, a reversible command, truthful feedback, and a receipt that states what remains unowned.


### 16. Existing authority inventory

The authority inventory for Mobile Medical Outreach is the current set of health, disease, medical ward, inventory, vehicle, expedition, consent, relationships, journal, and roster. Inspect each owner in source, data, save, host, and tests. The plan may reference a folded triage card, a sealed medicine tin, a vehicle cot strap, and a message receipt marked by hand and triage freshness, consent age, medicine custody, travel delay, symptom uncertainty, and follow-up route, but those observations become gameplay facts only when an existing owner emits them. Any older plan text that implies a new ledger, registry, or simulation is treated as a hypothesis until a current source path proves it.


### 17. Required delta

The smallest required delta is a consent-aware outreach dispatch projection over an existing care result, with clear limits between transport, referral, supply handoff, and clinical treatment. The delta does not include a new framework, a new global manager, or broad refactoring. It is one command or read projection, one representative row, one observable outcome, and one recovery path. The builder records the old behavior, the missing behavior, and the exact consumer that will make the change visible.


### 18. Premise and collision receipt

The premise receipt searches for alternate names, partial producers, retired implementations, stale IDs, test-only shims, and existing UI routes. Collision review is especially important for duplicated patient state, implied treatment, privacy leakage, medication double-spend, and unreachable referral branches. A collision is resolved by extending the current owner, adapting a current provider, or marking the proposal blocked. The receipt must state what was searched and which result is current.


### 19. Single-owner assignment

Assign simulation rules to Core, authored facts to JSON, persistence to the current save owner, presentation to Godot, input to the route, and validation to existing integrity gates. For Mobile Medical Outreach, the primary owner families are health and disease, ward duty, inventory, vehicle, expedition, consent, relationship, journal, and roster owners. If a value appears in more than one plan, the shared seam carries a canonical ID, source age, confidence, and event sequence instead of copied mutable state.


### 20. First safe player loop

The first safe loop for Mobile Medical Outreach is notice, inspect, compare, choose, observe, and recover. The proposed slice is one mobile referral packet that reads a current patient or settlement fact and routes an acknowledged, deferred, refused, or unresolved handoff. The player can verify, accept, defer, share, delegate, refuse, or return. Quiet success protects a routine, costly success spends a real resource, refusal preserves the earlier state, and unresolved play leaves a visible unknown. No timer, color, or sound cue is the sole proof of outcome.


### 21. Data contract

The representative data contract describes clinic visits, supply escorts, family messages, missed appointments, privacy choices, and return-to-care callbacks using stable snake_case IDs, schema_version, lifecycle, source, localization keys, references, bounded values, and correction notes. It distinguishes authored configuration, derived projection, and durable state. Duplicate IDs, missing references, unsupported fields, empty consumers, corrected rows, and old versions have explicit outcomes. A sample row must connect a folded triage card, a sealed medicine tin, a vehicle cot strap, and a message receipt marked by hand to an owner fact without turning the anchor into a second authority.


### 22. State ladder

The state ladder for Mobile Medical Outreach covers offered, inspected, accepted, active, paused, completed, failed, expired, corrected, and replay states only where the slice needs them. Every transition names the mutator, reservation, release rule, event, receipt, and interruption behavior. A repeated command is idempotent or refused. A correction adds evidence and preserves prior history. The ladder must make triage freshness, consent age, medicine custody, travel delay, symptom uncertainty, and follow-up route observable without storing duplicate counters.


### 23. Data flow

The data flow is INPUT → VALIDATION → CORE STATE or READ MODEL → DOMAIN EVENT → HOST PROVIDER → UI → PLAYER ACTION → COMMAND → CORE MUTATION → SAVE. For Mobile Medical Outreach, one mobile referral packet that reads a current patient or settlement fact and routes an acknowledged, deferred, refused, or unresolved handoff is the sample path. The flow identifies source age, confidence, stale behavior, and the delayed callback. Subscribers consume facts and request owner mutations; they never reach into foreign ledgers.


### 24. Save and migration

Capture only durable owner facts needed by Mobile Medical Outreach. Define CaptureState, RestoreState, version, old-save defaults, malformed-envelope refusal, checksum behavior, null and empty semantics, and deep-copy isolation. If the slice is a pure projection, say so and prove reconstruction after reload. A save during interruption must not duplicate rewards, penalties, messages, treatment, route results, archive entries, or relationship changes.


### 25. Determinism and replay

Use the seeded RNG contract only if the slice needs chance. Otherwise keep the result pure. Stable ordering for triage freshness, consent age, medicine custody, travel delay, symptom uncertainty, and follow-up route uses canonical IDs and authored ordinals. Pair same-seed inputs through save, reload, host reopen, duplicate delivery, interruption, and source correction. No wall-clock seed, process identity, random GUID, locale, or hash iteration order may affect the result. Record event order and final state hash.


### 26. Event and system wiring

Name the publisher, consumer, freshness, consequence owner, and delayed callback for every event. The preferred order is upstream world or owner fact, canonical result, projection, narrative callback, then UI refresh. Duplicate events are ignored or folded at the owner boundary. For Mobile Medical Outreach, cross-system callbacks may touch health, disease, medical ward, inventory, vehicle, expedition, consent, relationships, journal, and roster, but each callback must remain read-only until it asks the correct owner to mutate.


### 27. Godot host route

The host route exposes one mobile referral packet that reads a current patient or settlement fact and routes an acknowledged, deferred, refused, or unresolved handoff through the existing provider and panel lifecycle. It defines route registration, focus order, keyboard/controller bindings, refresh signal, disposal, close/back behavior, loading, empty, stale, blocked, redacted, complete, and error states. The panel does not count, reserve, score, or simulate. Optional visuals or audio degrade to text while preserving the command and a reasoned next action.


### 28. UI, UX, and accessibility

Accessibility acceptance for Mobile Medical Outreach includes readable text, scaling, contrast, non-color cues, reduced motion, sound-off alternatives, controller parity, focus retention, pagination, cancellation, and safe long strings. Uncertainty from triage freshness, consent age, medicine custody, travel delay, symptom uncertainty, and follow-up route is stated in text with source age. Redaction or consent blocks explain why content is unavailable. Refresh and disposal are idempotent, and a failed provider leaves the player with a recoverable state.


### 29. Narrative and side-quest integration

The content layer uses clinic visits, supply escorts, family messages, missed appointments, privacy choices, and return-to-care callbacks as grounded evidence. The representative branch has discovery, interpretation, complication, competing interest, escalation, irreversible choice, quiet outcome, costly outcome, refusal outcome, unresolved outcome, delayed callback, repeat policy, retirement rule, and correction behavior. Characters and locations use canonical IDs. Narrative text may reveal an observed owner fact but cannot create one.


### 30. Failure-mode attack

Attack Mobile Medical Outreach with null state, empty catalog, missing ID, duplicate ID, out-of-range value, stale source, corrected content, zero resources, dead actor, inaccessible route, completed quest, repeated event, huge value, negative value, unexpected event order, host reload, save during transition, corrupted save, deterministic replay, and unmounted UI. For each case, state whether the owner rejects, defaults, preserves old evidence, releases a reservation, or exposes a visible blocked state.


### 31. Performance and diagnostics

Set a bounded budget for Mobile Medical Outreach: first-page latency, refresh allocations, event fan-out, save delta, worst-case records, and cancellation. High-volume records are paged or lazy-loaded. Caches have one owner and invalidation. Diagnostics report source, consumer, age, and reason without storing private free text. A budget failure degrades to stale or empty presentation and never mutates authoritative state.


### 32. Focused test strategy

The focused strategy includes Core happy path, boundary, failure, invariant, event, and deterministic tests; save round-trip, deep-copy, old-version migration, invalid-version rejection, and checksum cases; data schema, duplicate-ID, reference, range, and consumer reachability checks; Core-to-provider and provider-to-Godot route checks; and a bounded headless check if the runtime path is affected. The first test file runs alone before aggregation.


### 33. Phase 0 premise audit

Phase 0 verifies the current premise for Mobile Medical Outreach. Inspect health and disease, ward duty, inventory, vehicle, expedition, consent, relationship, journal, and roster owners, current data files, routes, save sections, tests, and live claims. Record exact paths and stop if a required seam is missing or claimed by another package. Do not author production code, speculative data, or new tests during this phase unless the current foreman assigns them.


### 34. Phase 1 Core contract

Phase 1 adds the smallest Core command, query, DTO, or event needed for one mobile referral packet that reads a current patient or settlement fact and routes an acknowledged, deferred, refused, or unresolved handoff. It preserves engine independence and existing owner boundaries. The phase completes when invariants, duplicate handling, error results, and state transitions are covered. It must not add a new global registry, parallel ledger, or Godot dependency.


### 35. Phase 2 data and persistence

Phase 2 adds one validator-backed sample for clinic visits, supply escorts, family messages, missed appointments, privacy choices, and return-to-care callbacks, then proves migration or stateless reconstruction. Every field is classified as authored, derived, or durable. Save capture and restore are written before any claim of persistence. The phase must not widen the catalog or rename shared IDs until the sample is reachable and replay-stable.


### 36. Phase 3 host and route

Phase 3 binds the Core result to a thin host route. It preserves route registration, focus, close/back, refresh, disposal, error, stale, and empty behavior. The phase completes when a player can perform one mobile referral packet that reads a current patient or settlement fact and routes an acknowledged, deferred, refused, or unresolved handoff and see an observable owner result. It must not place domain calculations in the panel or create a local cache.


### 37. Phase 4 content and callbacks

Phase 4 adds one location or room, one named person or crew, one disturbance, and four outcome branches over the proven route. It connects to journal, archive, relationship, roster, route, shelter, or expedition facts only through current owners. It must not enable a content bank that has no runtime consumer.


### 38. File impact map

Likely areas are Assets/Ashfall.Core for owner contracts, Assets/StreamingAssets/Data for the representative row, src for provider and panel binding, and Ashfall.Core.Tests for focused contracts. Each path remains READ ONLY during premise audit and becomes MODIFY only after an exact claim is accepted. Generated outputs are changed only through their owning generator. No Unity-era path is a target.


### 39. Out of scope

Out of scope for Mobile Medical Outreach: a new global simulation, a second ledger, broad architecture cleanup, unrequested localization sweep, unrelated balance changes, retired Unity behavior, speculative save sections, private telemetry, and a global content browser. Nearby gaps remain visible in the blocker receipt and are not silently bundled.


### 40. Rollback and recovery

Rollback for Mobile Medical Outreach removes or disables the new adapter and content row while preserving old saves. A migration failure rejects the malformed envelope without partial mutation. A host failure unbinds the route while Core remains isolated. A content correction appends evidence. The smallest reversible package is one owner seam, one data row, one host route, one focused test set, and one handoff.


### 41. Definition of done

The slice is ready for implementation handoff when owner, consumer, data, state, save or stateless proof, deterministic replay, host route, accessibility, performance, content callback, focused verification, rollback, and exact file claims all have receipts. The package remains READY FOR PREMISE AUDIT until current evidence confirms the first gate. Word count and prose density never promote it.


### 42. Implementation handoff

The handoff for Mobile Medical Outreach names the objective, current reality, required delta, owner, data row, command, route, save impact, replay fixture, accessibility case, performance budget, content IDs, rollback, open blockers, and next bounded action. MUST PRESERVE: one owner, engine-free Core, JSON authority, deterministic behavior, current save ownership, truthful UI, and current ledger claims. MUST NOT DO: duplicate state, revive retired architecture, or claim integration from compile success alone.

### 20. First safe player loop

The first safe loop for Common Ground: Community Public Works is notice, inspect, compare, choose, observe, and recover. The proposed slice is one maintenance request for a current water or shelter asset with inspect, assign, defer, refuse, and completed-result callbacks. The player can verify, accept, defer, share, delegate, refuse, or return. Quiet success protects a routine, costly success spends a real resource, refusal preserves the earlier state, and unresolved play leaves a visible unknown. No timer, color, or sound cue is the sole proof of outcome.


### 21. Data contract

The representative data contract describes pump repairs, bridge crews, neighborhood disputes, public notices, resource tradeoffs, and reputation callbacks using stable snake_case IDs, schema_version, lifecycle, source, localization keys, references, bounded values, and correction notes. It distinguishes authored configuration, derived projection, and durable state. Duplicate IDs, missing references, unsupported fields, empty consumers, corrected rows, and old versions have explicit outcomes. A sample row must connect a chalked intake valve, a patched bridge plank, a bucket tally, and a public notice pinned under glass to an owner fact without turning the anchor into a second authority.


### 22. State ladder

The state ladder for Common Ground: Community Public Works covers offered, inspected, accepted, active, paused, completed, failed, expired, corrected, and replay states only where the slice needs them. Every transition names the mutator, reservation, release rule, event, receipt, and interruption behavior. A repeated command is idempotent or refused. A correction adds evidence and preserves prior history. The ladder must make flow confidence, work age, crew availability, public trust, repair risk, and service callback observable without storing duplicate counters.


### 23. Data flow

The data flow is INPUT → VALIDATION → CORE STATE or READ MODEL → DOMAIN EVENT → HOST PROVIDER → UI → PLAYER ACTION → COMMAND → CORE MUTATION → SAVE. For Common Ground: Community Public Works, one maintenance request for a current water or shelter asset with inspect, assign, defer, refuse, and completed-result callbacks is the sample path. The flow identifies source age, confidence, stale behavior, and the delayed callback. Subscribers consume facts and request owner mutations; they never reach into foreign ledgers.


### 24. Save and migration

Capture only durable owner facts needed by Common Ground: Community Public Works. Define CaptureState, RestoreState, version, old-save defaults, malformed-envelope refusal, checksum behavior, null and empty semantics, and deep-copy isolation. If the slice is a pure projection, say so and prove reconstruction after reload. A save during interruption must not duplicate rewards, penalties, messages, treatment, route results, archive entries, or relationship changes.


### 25. Determinism and replay

Use the seeded RNG contract only if the slice needs chance. Otherwise keep the result pure. Stable ordering for flow confidence, work age, crew availability, public trust, repair risk, and service callback uses canonical IDs and authored ordinals. Pair same-seed inputs through save, reload, host reopen, duplicate delivery, interruption, and source correction. No wall-clock seed, process identity, random GUID, locale, or hash iteration order may affect the result. Record event order and final state hash.


### 26. Event and system wiring

Name the publisher, consumer, freshness, consequence owner, and delayed callback for every event. The preferred order is upstream world or owner fact, canonical result, projection, narrative callback, then UI refresh. Duplicate events are ignored or folded at the owner boundary. For Common Ground: Community Public Works, cross-system callbacks may touch water, projects, settlement, territory, colony, reputation, press, archive, and civic labor, but each callback must remain read-only until it asks the correct owner to mutate.


### 27. Godot host route

The host route exposes one maintenance request for a current water or shelter asset with inspect, assign, defer, refuse, and completed-result callbacks through the existing provider and panel lifecycle. It defines route registration, focus order, keyboard/controller bindings, refresh signal, disposal, close/back behavior, loading, empty, stale, blocked, redacted, complete, and error states. The panel does not count, reserve, score, or simulate. Optional visuals or audio degrade to text while preserving the command and a reasoned next action.


### 28. UI, UX, and accessibility

Accessibility acceptance for Common Ground: Community Public Works includes readable text, scaling, contrast, non-color cues, reduced motion, sound-off alternatives, controller parity, focus retention, pagination, cancellation, and safe long strings. Uncertainty from flow confidence, work age, crew availability, public trust, repair risk, and service callback is stated in text with source age. Redaction or consent blocks explain why content is unavailable. Refresh and disposal are idempotent, and a failed provider leaves the player with a recoverable state.


### 29. Narrative and side-quest integration

The content layer uses pump repairs, bridge crews, neighborhood disputes, public notices, resource tradeoffs, and reputation callbacks as grounded evidence. The representative branch has discovery, interpretation, complication, competing interest, escalation, irreversible choice, quiet outcome, costly outcome, refusal outcome, unresolved outcome, delayed callback, repeat policy, retirement rule, and correction behavior. Characters and locations use canonical IDs. Narrative text may reveal an observed owner fact but cannot create one.


### 30. Failure-mode attack

Attack Common Ground: Community Public Works with null state, empty catalog, missing ID, duplicate ID, out-of-range value, stale source, corrected content, zero resources, dead actor, inaccessible route, completed quest, repeated event, huge value, negative value, unexpected event order, host reload, save during transition, corrupted save, deterministic replay, and unmounted UI. For each case, state whether the owner rejects, defaults, preserves old evidence, releases a reservation, or exposes a visible blocked state.


### 31. Performance and diagnostics

Set a bounded budget for Common Ground: Community Public Works: first-page latency, refresh allocations, event fan-out, save delta, worst-case records, and cancellation. High-volume records are paged or lazy-loaded. Caches have one owner and invalidation. Diagnostics report source, consumer, age, and reason without storing private free text. A budget failure degrades to stale or empty presentation and never mutates authoritative state.


### 32. Focused test strategy

The focused strategy includes Core happy path, boundary, failure, invariant, event, and deterministic tests; save round-trip, deep-copy, old-version migration, invalid-version rejection, and checksum cases; data schema, duplicate-ID, reference, range, and consumer reachability checks; Core-to-provider and provider-to-Godot route checks; and a bounded headless check if the runtime path is affected. The first test file runs alone before aggregation.


### 33. Phase 0 premise audit

Phase 0 verifies the current premise for Common Ground: Community Public Works. Inspect water treatment, fluid delivery, project, territory, settlement, colony, reputation, press, and archive owners, current data files, routes, save sections, tests, and live claims. Record exact paths and stop if a required seam is missing or claimed by another package. Do not author production code, speculative data, or new tests during this phase unless the current foreman assigns them.


### 34. Phase 1 Core contract

Phase 1 adds the smallest Core command, query, DTO, or event needed for one maintenance request for a current water or shelter asset with inspect, assign, defer, refuse, and completed-result callbacks. It preserves engine independence and existing owner boundaries. The phase completes when invariants, duplicate handling, error results, and state transitions are covered. It must not add a new global registry, parallel ledger, or Godot dependency.


### 35. Phase 2 data and persistence

Phase 2 adds one validator-backed sample for pump repairs, bridge crews, neighborhood disputes, public notices, resource tradeoffs, and reputation callbacks, then proves migration or stateless reconstruction. Every field is classified as authored, derived, or durable. Save capture and restore are written before any claim of persistence. The phase must not widen the catalog or rename shared IDs until the sample is reachable and replay-stable.


### 36. Phase 3 host and route

Phase 3 binds the Core result to a thin host route. It preserves route registration, focus, close/back, refresh, disposal, error, stale, and empty behavior. The phase completes when a player can perform one maintenance request for a current water or shelter asset with inspect, assign, defer, refuse, and completed-result callbacks and see an observable owner result. It must not place domain calculations in the panel or create a local cache.


### 37. Phase 4 content and callbacks

Phase 4 adds one location or room, one named person or crew, one disturbance, and four outcome branches over the proven route. It connects to journal, archive, relationship, roster, route, shelter, or expedition facts only through current owners. It must not enable a content bank that has no runtime consumer.


### 38. File impact map

Likely areas are Assets/Ashfall.Core for owner contracts, Assets/StreamingAssets/Data for the representative row, src for provider and panel binding, and Ashfall.Core.Tests for focused contracts. Each path remains READ ONLY during premise audit and becomes MODIFY only after an exact claim is accepted. Generated outputs are changed only through their owning generator. No Unity-era path is a target.


### 39. Out of scope

Out of scope for Common Ground: Community Public Works: a new global simulation, a second ledger, broad architecture cleanup, unrequested localization sweep, unrelated balance changes, retired Unity behavior, speculative save sections, private telemetry, and a global content browser. Nearby gaps remain visible in the blocker receipt and are not silently bundled.


### 40. Rollback and recovery

Rollback for Common Ground: Community Public Works removes or disables the new adapter and content row while preserving old saves. A migration failure rejects the malformed envelope without partial mutation. A host failure unbinds the route while Core remains isolated. A content correction appends evidence. The smallest reversible package is one owner seam, one data row, one host route, one focused test set, and one handoff.


### 41. Definition of done

The slice is ready for implementation handoff when owner, consumer, data, state, save or stateless proof, deterministic replay, host route, accessibility, performance, content callback, focused verification, rollback, and exact file claims all have receipts. The package remains READY FOR PREMISE AUDIT until current evidence confirms the first gate. Word count and prose density never promote it.


### 42. Implementation handoff

The handoff for Common Ground: Community Public Works names the objective, current reality, required delta, owner, data row, command, route, save impact, replay fixture, accessibility case, performance budget, content IDs, rollback, open blockers, and next bounded action. MUST PRESERVE: one owner, engine-free Core, JSON authority, deterministic behavior, current save ownership, truthful UI, and current ledger claims. MUST NOT DO: duplicate state, revive retired architecture, or claim integration from compile success alone.

## Plan 4 — Scenario Campaign Library — closeout dossier


**Program band:** narrative questline, campaign manifest, difficulty, identity, localization, save, ModSupport, and scenario content
**Primary owners:** NarrativeQuestlineSystem, campaign manifest, difficulty selector, shelter identity, localization, save, and ModSupport owners
**Required delta:** a bounded scenario package route over current campaign and narrative authorities, with explicit package lifecycle and no global scenario browser until a signed catalog exists
**First safe slice:** one anthology package with a manifest-valid entry point, a choice, a delayed callback, and a clean retirement path
**Material anchors:** a scenario folder tied in red thread, a shelter token, a stamped difficulty card, and an ending note
**Observable signals:** package version, branch reachability, identity context, choice freshness, callback status, and retirement state
**Content family:** anthology chapters, authored characters, scenario openings, branch consequences, and ending fragments
**Primary risks:** parallel campaign manager, fake scenario loading, cross-run leakage, missing localization, and unsupported mod fields

This dossier closes the planning surface for Plan 4 without claiming runtime integration. Every subsection is a handoff requirement, not an implementation result.

### 15. Closeout objective

Close Scenario Campaign Library as a premise-gated package. The objective is to turn a bounded scenario package route over current campaign and narrative authorities, with explicit package lifecycle and no global scenario browser until a signed catalog exists into one reviewable vertical slice while preserving NarrativeQuestlineSystem, campaign manifest, difficulty selector, shelter identity, localization, save, and ModSupport owners. The package is successful when the first slice has a named publisher, a consumer that can be reached by a current route, a reversible command, truthful feedback, and a receipt that states what remains unowned.

Nearby gaps remain visible in the blocker receipt and are not silently bundled.

Rollback for Scenario Campaign Library removes or disables the new adapter and content row while preserving old saves. A migration failure rejects the malformed envelope without partial mutation. A host failure unbinds the route while Core remains isolated. A content correction appends evidence. The smallest reversible package is one owner seam, one data row, one host route, one focused test set, and one handoff.

The slice is ready for implementation handoff when owner, consumer, data, state, save or stateless proof, deterministic replay, host route, accessibility, performance, content callback, focused verification, rollback, and exact file claims all have receipts. The package remains READY FOR PREMISE AUDIT until current evidence confirms the first gate. Word count and prose density never promote it.

## Plan 5 — Regional Supply and Travel Resilience — closeout dossier


**Program band:** route, cargo, settlement, weather, vehicle, communications, expedition, trade, and shelter
**Primary owners:** route, cargo, settlement, weather, vehicle, communications, expedition, trade, and shelter owners
**Required delta:** an arrival-proof read model over an existing route result, exposing cargo reservation, delay, confidence, and recovery without a second travel or trade ledger
**First safe slice:** one waystation arrival with a weather delay, shared-load choice, and journal or settlement callback
**Material anchors:** a waystation chalk mark, a cargo seal, a wet route card, and a lantern receipt
**Observable signals:** arrival confidence, cargo custody, weather age, crew strain, settlement need, and return route
**Content family:** waystations, convoy decisions, shared loads, storm delays, trade promises, and return-leg callbacks
**Primary risks:** duplicate route graph, cargo double-spend, weather re-simulation, settlement counter drift, and stale arrival UI

This dossier closes the planning surface for Plan 5 without claiming runtime integration. Every subsection is a handoff requirement, not an implementation result.

### 15. Closeout objective

Close Regional Supply and Travel Resilience as a premise-gated package. The objective is to turn an arrival-proof read model over an existing route result, exposing cargo reservation, delay, confidence, and recovery without a second travel or trade ledger into one reviewable vertical slice while preserving route, cargo, settlement, weather, vehicle, communications, expedition, trade, and shelter owners. The package is successful when the first slice has a named publisher, a consumer that can be reached by a current route, a reversible command, truthful feedback, and a receipt that states what remains unowned.


### 16. Existing authority inventory

The authority inventory for Regional Supply and Travel Resilience is the current set of route, cargo, settlement, weather, vehicle, communications, expedition, trade, and shelter. Inspect each owner in source, data, save, host, and tests. The plan may reference a waystation chalk mark, a cargo seal, a wet route card, and a lantern receipt and arrival confidence, cargo custody, weather age, crew strain, settlement need, and return route, but those observations become gameplay facts only when an existing owner emits them. Any older plan text that implies a new ledger, registry, or simulation is treated as a hypothesis until a current source path proves it.


### 17. Required delta

The smallest required delta is an arrival-proof read model over an existing route result, exposing cargo reservation, delay, confidence, and recovery without a second travel or trade ledger. The delta does not include a new framework, a new global manager, or broad refactoring. It is one command or read projection, one representative row, one observable outcome, and one recovery path. The builder records the old behavior, the missing behavior, and the exact consumer that will make the change visible.


### 18. Premise and collision receipt

The premise receipt searches for alternate names, partial producers, retired implementations, stale IDs, test-only shims, and existing UI routes. Collision review is especially important for duplicate route graph, cargo double-spend, weather re-simulation, settlement counter drift, and stale arrival UI. A collision is resolved by extending the current owner, adapting a current provider, or marking the proposal blocked. The receipt must state what was searched and which result is current.


### 19. Single-owner assignment

Assign simulation rules to Core, authored facts to JSON, persistence to the current save owner, presentation to Godot, input to the route, and validation to existing integrity gates. For Regional Supply and Travel Resilience, the primary owner families are route, cargo, settlement, weather, vehicle, communications, expedition, trade, and shelter owners. If a value appears in more than one plan, the shared seam carries a canonical ID, source age, confidence, and event sequence instead of copied mutable state.


### 20. First safe player loop

The first safe loop for Regional Supply and Travel Resilience is notice, inspect, compare, choose, observe, and recover. The proposed slice is one waystation arrival with a weather delay, shared-load choice, and journal or settlement callback. The player can verify, accept, defer, share, delegate, refuse, or return. Quiet success protects a routine, costly success spends a real resource, refusal preserves the earlier state, and unresolved play leaves a visible unknown. No timer, color, or sound cue is the sole proof of outcome.


### 21. Data contract

The representative data contract describes waystations, convoy decisions, shared loads, storm delays, trade promises, and return-leg callbacks using stable snake_case IDs, schema_version, lifecycle, source, localization keys, references, bounded values, and correction notes. It distinguishes authored configuration, derived projection, and durable state. Duplicate IDs, missing references, unsupported fields, empty consumers, corrected rows, and old versions have explicit outcomes. A sample row must connect a waystation chalk mark, a cargo seal, a wet route card, and a lantern receipt to an owner fact without turning the anchor into a second authority.


### 22. State ladder

The state ladder for Regional Supply and Travel Resilience covers offered, inspected, accepted, active, paused, completed, failed, expired, corrected, and replay states only where the slice needs them. Every transition names the mutator, reservation, release rule, event, receipt, and interruption behavior. A repeated command is idempotent or refused. A correction adds evidence and preserves prior history. The ladder must make arrival confidence, cargo custody, weather age, crew strain, settlement need, and return route observable without storing duplicate counters.


### 23. Data flow

The data flow is INPUT → VALIDATION → CORE STATE or READ MODEL → DOMAIN EVENT → HOST PROVIDER → UI → PLAYER ACTION → COMMAND → CORE MUTATION → SAVE. For Regional Supply and Travel Resilience, one waystation arrival with a weather delay, shared-load choice, and journal or settlement callback is the sample path. The flow identifies source age, confidence, stale behavior, and the delayed callback. Subscribers consume facts and request owner mutations; they never reach into foreign ledgers.


### 24. Save and migration

Capture only durable owner facts needed by Regional Supply and Travel Resilience. Define CaptureState, RestoreState, version, old-save defaults, malformed-envelope refusal, checksum behavior, null and empty semantics, and deep-copy isolation. If the slice is a pure projection, say so and prove reconstruction after reload. A save during interruption must not duplicate rewards, penalties, messages, treatment, route results, archive entries, or relationship changes.


### 25. Determinism and replay

Use the seeded RNG contract only if the slice needs chance. Otherwise keep the result pure. Stable ordering for arrival confidence, cargo custody, weather age, crew strain, settlement need, and return route uses canonical IDs and authored ordinals. Pair same-seed inputs through save, reload, host reopen, duplicate delivery, interruption, and source correction. No wall-clock seed, process identity, random GUID, locale, or hash iteration order may affect the result. Record event order and final state hash.


### 26. Event and system wiring

Name the publisher, consumer, freshness, consequence owner, and delayed callback for every event. The preferred order is upstream world or owner fact, canonical result, projection, narrative callback, then UI refresh. Duplicate events are ignored or folded at the owner boundary. For Regional Supply and Travel Resilience, cross-system callbacks may touch route, cargo, settlement, weather, vehicle, communications, expedition, trade, and shelter, but each callback must remain read-only until it asks the correct owner to mutate.


### 27. Godot host route

The host route exposes one waystation arrival with a weather delay, shared-load choice, and journal or settlement callback through the existing provider and panel lifecycle. It defines route registration, focus order, keyboard/controller bindings, refresh signal, disposal, close/back behavior, loading, empty, stale, blocked, redacted, complete, and error states. The panel does not count, reserve, score, or simulate. Optional visuals or audio degrade to text while preserving the command and a reasoned next action.


### 28. UI, UX, and accessibility

Accessibility acceptance for Regional Supply and Travel Resilience includes readable text, scaling, contrast, non-color cues, reduced motion, sound-off alternatives, controller parity, focus retention, pagination, cancellation, and safe long strings. Uncertainty from arrival confidence, cargo custody, weather age, crew strain, settlement need, and return route is stated in text with source age. Redaction or consent blocks explain why content is unavailable. Refresh and disposal are idempotent, and a failed provider leaves the player with a recoverable state.


### 29. Narrative and side-quest integration

The content layer uses waystations, convoy decisions, shared loads, storm delays, trade promises, and return-leg callbacks as grounded evidence. The representative branch has discovery, interpretation, complication, competing interest, escalation, irreversible choice, quiet outcome, costly outcome, refusal outcome, unresolved outcome, delayed callback, repeat policy, retirement rule, and correction behavior. Characters and locations use canonical IDs. Narrative text may reveal an observed owner fact but cannot create one.


### 30. Failure-mode attack

Attack Regional Supply and Travel Resilience with null state, empty catalog, missing ID, duplicate ID, out-of-range value, stale source, corrected content, zero resources, dead actor, inaccessible route, completed quest, repeated event, huge value, negative value, unexpected event order, host reload, save during transition, corrupted save, deterministic replay, and unmounted UI. For each case, state whether the owner rejects, defaults, preserves old evidence, releases a reservation, or exposes a visible blocked state.


### 31. Performance and diagnostics

Set a bounded budget for Regional Supply and Travel Resilience: first-page latency, refresh allocations, event fan-out, save delta, worst-case records, and cancellation. High-volume records are paged or lazy-loaded. Caches have one owner and invalidation. Diagnostics report source, consumer, age, and reason without storing private free text. A budget failure degrades to stale or empty presentation and never mutates authoritative state.


### 32. Focused test strategy

The focused strategy includes Core happy path, boundary, failure, invariant, event, and deterministic tests; save round-trip, deep-copy, old-version migration, invalid-version rejection, and checksum cases; data schema, duplicate-ID, reference, range, and consumer reachability checks; Core-to-provider and provider-to-Godot route checks; and a bounded headless check if the runtime path is affected. The first test file runs alone before aggregation.


### 33. Phase 0 premise audit

Phase 0 verifies the current premise for Regional Supply and Travel Resilience. Inspect route, cargo, settlement, weather, vehicle, communications, expedition, trade, and shelter owners, current data files, routes, save sections, tests, and live claims. Record exact paths and stop if a required seam is missing or claimed by another package. Do not author production code, speculative data, or new tests during this phase unless the current foreman assigns them.


### 34. Phase 1 Core contract

Phase 1 adds the smallest Core command, query, DTO, or event needed for one waystation arrival with a weather delay, shared-load choice, and journal or settlement callback. It preserves engine independence and existing owner boundaries. The phase completes when invariants, duplicate handling, error results, and state transitions are covered. It must not add a new global registry, parallel ledger, or Godot dependency.


### 35. Phase 2 data and persistence

Phase 2 adds one validator-backed sample for waystations, convoy decisions, shared loads, storm delays, trade promises, and return-leg callbacks, then proves migration or stateless reconstruction. Every field is classified as authored, derived, or durable. Save capture and restore are written before any claim of persistence. The phase must not widen the catalog or rename shared IDs until the sample is reachable and replay-stable.


### 36. Phase 3 host and route

Phase 3 binds the Core result to a thin host route. It preserves route registration, focus, close/back, refresh, disposal, error, stale, and empty behavior. The phase completes when a player can perform one waystation arrival with a weather delay, shared-load choice, and journal or settlement callback and see an observable owner result. It must not place domain calculations in the panel or create a local cache.


### 37. Phase 4 content and callbacks

Phase 4 adds one location or room, one named person or crew, one disturbance, and four outcome branches over the proven route. It connects to journal, archive, relationship, roster, route, shelter, or expedition facts only through current owners. It must not enable a content bank that has no runtime consumer.

Each path remains READ ONLY during premise audit and becomes MODIFY only after an exact claim is accepted. Generated outputs are changed only through their owning generator. No Unity-era path is a target.


### 39. Out of scope

Out of scope for Regional Supply and Travel Resilience: a new global simulation, a second ledger, broad architecture cleanup, unrequested localization sweep, unrelated balance changes, retired Unity behavior, speculative save sections, private telemetry, and a global content browser.
