## Plan 6 — Survivor Relationship and Memory Network — closeout dossier


**Program band:** relationship, journal, consent, identity, roster, time-capsule, archive, and social consequence
**Primary owners:** relationship, journal, consent, identity, roster, time-capsule, archive, and social consequence owners
**Required delta:** a consent-aware memory projection over existing relationship or journal facts, with redaction and renewal commands owned by the current systems
**First safe slice:** one remembered promise that can be confirmed, deferred, redacted, or left unresolved and later appears as a journal projection
**Material anchors:** a promise card, a worn photograph sleeve, a name token, and a note sealed for later
**Observable signals:** memory source, consent age, trust change, identity confidence, redaction state, and callback date
**Content family:** promises, reconciliations, private letters, witness disputes, memorial ties, and renewed consent
**Primary risks:** second relationship graph, private-data leak, memory decay authority, duplicate affinity write, and coercive UI

This dossier closes the planning surface for Plan 6 without claiming runtime integration. Every subsection is a handoff requirement, not an implementation result.

### 15. Closeout objective

Close Survivor Relationship and Memory Network as a premise-gated package. The objective is to turn a consent-aware memory projection over existing relationship or journal facts, with redaction and renewal commands owned by the current systems into one reviewable vertical slice while preserving relationship, journal, consent, identity, roster, time-capsule, archive, and social consequence owners. The package is successful when the first slice has a named publisher, a consumer that can be reached by a current route, a reversible command, truthful feedback, and a receipt that states what remains unowned.


### 16. Existing authority inventory

The authority inventory for Survivor Relationship and Memory Network is the current set of relationship, journal, consent, identity, roster, time-capsule, archive, and social consequence. Inspect each owner in source, data, save, host, and tests. The plan may reference a promise card, a worn photograph sleeve, a name token, and a note sealed for later and memory source, consent age, trust change, identity confidence, redaction state, and callback date, but those observations become gameplay facts only when an existing owner emits them. Any older plan text that implies a new ledger, registry, or simulation is treated as a hypothesis until a current source path proves it.


### 17. Required delta

The smallest required delta is a consent-aware memory projection over existing relationship or journal facts, with redaction and renewal commands owned by the current systems. The delta does not include a new framework, a new global manager, or broad refactoring. It is one command or read projection, one representative row, one observable outcome, and one recovery path. The builder records the old behavior, the missing behavior, and the exact consumer that will make the change visible.


### 18. Premise and collision receipt

The premise receipt searches for alternate names, partial producers, retired implementations, stale IDs, test-only shims, and existing UI routes. Collision review is especially important for second relationship graph, private-data leak, memory decay authority, duplicate affinity write, and coercive UI. A collision is resolved by extending the current owner, adapting a current provider, or marking the proposal blocked. The receipt must state what was searched and which result is current.


### 19. Single-owner assignment

Assign simulation rules to Core, authored facts to JSON, persistence to the current save owner, presentation to Godot, input to the route, and validation to existing integrity gates. For Survivor Relationship and Memory Network, the primary owner families are relationship, journal, consent, identity, roster, time-capsule, archive, and social consequence owners. If a value appears in more than one plan, the shared seam carries a canonical ID, source age, confidence, and event sequence instead of copied mutable state.


### 20. First safe player loop

The first safe loop for Survivor Relationship and Memory Network is notice, inspect, compare, choose, observe, and recover. The proposed slice is one remembered promise that can be confirmed, deferred, redacted, or left unresolved and later appears as a journal projection. The player can verify, accept, defer, share, delegate, refuse, or return. Quiet success protects a routine, costly success spends a real resource, refusal preserves the earlier state, and unresolved play leaves a visible unknown. No timer, color, or sound cue is the sole proof of outcome.


### 21. Data contract

The representative data contract describes promises, reconciliations, private letters, witness disputes, memorial ties, and renewed consent using stable snake_case IDs, schema_version, lifecycle, source, localization keys, references, bounded values, and correction notes. It distinguishes authored configuration, derived projection, and durable state. Duplicate IDs, missing references, unsupported fields, empty consumers, corrected rows, and old versions have explicit outcomes. A sample row must connect a promise card, a worn photograph sleeve, a name token, and a note sealed for later to an owner fact without turning the anchor into a second authority.


### 22. State ladder

The state ladder for Survivor Relationship and Memory Network covers offered, inspected, accepted, active, paused, completed, failed, expired, corrected, and replay states only where the slice needs them. Every transition names the mutator, reservation, release rule, event, receipt, and interruption behavior. A repeated command is idempotent or refused. A correction adds evidence and preserves prior history. The ladder must make memory source, consent age, trust change, identity confidence, redaction state, and callback date observable without storing duplicate counters.


### 23. Data flow

The data flow is INPUT → VALIDATION → CORE STATE or READ MODEL → DOMAIN EVENT → HOST PROVIDER → UI → PLAYER ACTION → COMMAND → CORE MUTATION → SAVE. For Survivor Relationship and Memory Network, one remembered promise that can be confirmed, deferred, redacted, or left unresolved and later appears as a journal projection is the sample path. The flow identifies source age, confidence, stale behavior, and the delayed callback. Subscribers consume facts and request owner mutations; they never reach into foreign ledgers.


### 24. Save and migration

Capture only durable owner facts needed by Survivor Relationship and Memory Network. Define CaptureState, RestoreState, version, old-save defaults, malformed-envelope refusal, checksum behavior, null and empty semantics, and deep-copy isolation. If the slice is a pure projection, say so and prove reconstruction after reload. A save during interruption must not duplicate rewards, penalties, messages, treatment, route results, archive entries, or relationship changes.


### 25. Determinism and replay

Use the seeded RNG contract only if the slice needs chance. Otherwise keep the result pure. Stable ordering for memory source, consent age, trust change, identity confidence, redaction state, and callback date uses canonical IDs and authored ordinals. Pair same-seed inputs through save, reload, host reopen, duplicate delivery, interruption, and source correction. No wall-clock seed, process identity, random GUID, locale, or hash iteration order may affect the result. Record event order and final state hash.


### 26. Event and system wiring

Name the publisher, consumer, freshness, consequence owner, and delayed callback for every event. The preferred order is upstream world or owner fact, canonical result, projection, narrative callback, then UI refresh. Duplicate events are ignored or folded at the owner boundary. For Survivor Relationship and Memory Network, cross-system callbacks may touch relationship, journal, consent, identity, roster, time-capsule, archive, and social consequence, but each callback must remain read-only until it asks the correct owner to mutate.


### 27. Godot host route

The host route exposes one remembered promise that can be confirmed, deferred, redacted, or left unresolved and later appears as a journal projection through the existing provider and panel lifecycle. It defines route registration, focus order, keyboard/controller bindings, refresh signal, disposal, close/back behavior, loading, empty, stale, blocked, redacted, complete, and error states. The panel does not count, reserve, score, or simulate. Optional visuals or audio degrade to text while preserving the command and a reasoned next action.


### 28. UI, UX, and accessibility

Accessibility acceptance for Survivor Relationship and Memory Network includes readable text, scaling, contrast, non-color cues, reduced motion, sound-off alternatives, controller parity, focus retention, pagination, cancellation, and safe long strings. Uncertainty from memory source, consent age, trust change, identity confidence, redaction state, and callback date is stated in text with source age. Redaction or consent blocks explain why content is unavailable. Refresh and disposal are idempotent, and a failed provider leaves the player with a recoverable state.


### 29. Narrative and side-quest integration

The content layer uses promises, reconciliations, private letters, witness disputes, memorial ties, and renewed consent as grounded evidence. The representative branch has discovery, interpretation, complication, competing interest, escalation, irreversible choice, quiet outcome, costly outcome, refusal outcome, unresolved outcome, delayed callback, repeat policy, retirement rule, and correction behavior. Characters and locations use canonical IDs. Narrative text may reveal an observed owner fact but cannot create one.


### 30. Failure-mode attack

Attack Survivor Relationship and Memory Network with null state, empty catalog, missing ID, duplicate ID, out-of-range value, stale source, corrected content, zero resources, dead actor, inaccessible route, completed quest, repeated event, huge value, negative value, unexpected event order, host reload, save during transition, corrupted save, deterministic replay, and unmounted UI. For each case, state whether the owner rejects, defaults, preserves old evidence, releases a reservation, or exposes a visible blocked state.


### 31. Performance and diagnostics

Set a bounded budget for Survivor Relationship and Memory Network: first-page latency, refresh allocations, event fan-out, save delta, worst-case records, and cancellation. High-volume records are paged or lazy-loaded. Caches have one owner and invalidation. Diagnostics report source, consumer, age, and reason without storing private free text. A budget failure degrades to stale or empty presentation and never mutates authoritative state.


### 32. Focused test strategy

The focused strategy includes Core happy path, boundary, failure, invariant, event, and deterministic tests; save round-trip, deep-copy, old-version migration, invalid-version rejection, and checksum cases; data schema, duplicate-ID, reference, range, and consumer reachability checks; Core-to-provider and provider-to-Godot route checks; and a bounded headless check if the runtime path is affected. The first test file runs alone before aggregation.


### 33. Phase 0 premise audit

Phase 0 verifies the current premise for Survivor Relationship and Memory Network. Inspect relationship, journal, consent, identity, roster, time-capsule, archive, and social consequence owners, current data files, routes, save sections, tests, and live claims. Record exact paths and stop if a required seam is missing or claimed by another package. Do not author production code, speculative data, or new tests during this phase unless the current foreman assigns them.


### 34. Phase 1 Core contract

Phase 1 adds the smallest Core command, query, DTO, or event needed for one remembered promise that can be confirmed, deferred, redacted, or left unresolved and later appears as a journal projection. It preserves engine independence and existing owner boundaries. The phase completes when invariants, duplicate handling, error results, and state transitions are covered. It must not add a new global registry, parallel ledger, or Godot dependency.


### 35. Phase 2 data and persistence

Phase 2 adds one validator-backed sample for promises, reconciliations, private letters, witness disputes, memorial ties, and renewed consent, then proves migration or stateless reconstruction. Every field is classified as authored, derived, or durable. Save capture and restore are written before any claim of persistence. The phase must not widen the catalog or rename shared IDs until the sample is reachable and replay-stable.


### 36. Phase 3 host and route

Phase 3 binds the Core result to a thin host route. It preserves route registration, focus, close/back, refresh, disposal, error, stale, and empty behavior. The phase completes when a player can perform one remembered promise that can be confirmed, deferred, redacted, or left unresolved and later appears as a journal projection and see an observable owner result. It must not place domain calculations in the panel or create a local cache.


### 37. Phase 4 content and callbacks

Phase 4 adds one location or room, one named person or crew, one disturbance, and four outcome branches over the proven route. It connects to journal, archive, relationship, roster, route, shelter, or expedition facts only through current owners. It must not enable a content bank that has no runtime consumer.


### 38. File impact map

Likely areas are Assets/Ashfall.Core for owner contracts, Assets/StreamingAssets/Data for the representative row, src for provider and panel binding, and Ashfall.Core.Tests for focused contracts. Each path remains READ ONLY during premise audit and becomes MODIFY only after an exact claim is accepted. Generated outputs are changed only through their owning generator. No Unity-era path is a target.


### 39. Out of scope

Out of scope for Survivor Relationship and Memory Network: a new global simulation, a second ledger, broad architecture cleanup, unrequested localization sweep, unrelated balance changes, retired Unity behavior, speculative save sections, private telemetry, and a global content browser. Nearby gaps remain visible in the blocker receipt and are not silently bundled.


### 40. Rollback and recovery

Rollback for Survivor Relationship and Memory Network removes or disables the new adapter and content row while preserving old saves. A migration failure rejects the malformed envelope without partial mutation. A host failure unbinds the route while Core remains isolated. A content correction appends evidence. The smallest reversible package is one owner seam, one data row, one host route, one focused test set, and one handoff.


### 41. Definition of done

The slice is ready for implementation handoff when owner, consumer, data, state, save or stateless proof, deterministic replay, host route, accessibility, performance, content callback, focused verification, rollback, and exact file claims all have receipts. The package remains READY FOR PREMISE AUDIT until current evidence confirms the first gate. Word count and prose density never promote it.


### 42. Implementation handoff

The handoff for Survivor Relationship and Memory Network names the objective, current reality, required delta, owner, data row, command, route, save impact, replay fixture, accessibility case, performance budget, content IDs, rollback, open blockers, and next bounded action. MUST PRESERVE: one owner, engine-free Core, JSON authority, deterministic behavior, current save ownership, truthful UI, and current ledger claims. MUST NOT DO: duplicate state, revive retired architecture, or claim integration from compile success alone.

The representative data contract describes cold rooms, repair shifts, rationed heat, duty conflicts, accessible warnings, and reserve callbacks using stable snake_case IDs, schema_version, lifecycle, source, localization keys, references, bounded values, and correction notes. It distinguishes authored configuration, derived projection, and durable state. Duplicate IDs, missing references, unsupported fields, empty consumers, corrected rows, and old versions have explicit outcomes. A sample row must connect a breaker label, a frost gauge, a water pressure slip, and a duty roster clip to an owner fact without turning the anchor into a second authority.


### 22. State ladder

The state ladder for Shelter Automation and Power Recovery covers offered, inspected, accepted, active, paused, completed, failed, expired, corrected, and replay states only where the slice needs them. Every transition names the mutator, reservation, release rule, event, receipt, and interruption behavior. A repeated command is idempotent or refused. A correction adds evidence and preserves prior history. The ladder must make reserve margin, room demand, warmth age, duty coverage, water pressure, and recovery time observable without storing duplicate counters.


### 23. Data flow

The data flow is INPUT → VALIDATION → CORE STATE or READ MODEL → DOMAIN EVENT → HOST PROVIDER → UI → PLAYER ACTION → COMMAND → CORE MUTATION → SAVE. For Shelter Automation and Power Recovery, one cold-room demand spike resolved through a current owner event with reserve explanation and accessible warning is the sample path. The flow identifies source age, confidence, stale behavior, and the delayed callback. Subscribers consume facts and request owner mutations; they never reach into foreign ledgers.


### 24. Save and migration

Capture only durable owner facts needed by Shelter Automation and Power Recovery. Define CaptureState, RestoreState, version, old-save defaults, malformed-envelope refusal, checksum behavior, null and empty semantics, and deep-copy isolation. If the slice is a pure projection, say so and prove reconstruction after reload. A save during interruption must not duplicate rewards, penalties, messages, treatment, route results, archive entries, or relationship changes.


### 25. Determinism and replay

Use the seeded RNG contract only if the slice needs chance. Otherwise keep the result pure. Stable ordering for reserve margin, room demand, warmth age, duty coverage, water pressure, and recovery time uses canonical IDs and authored ordinals. Pair same-seed inputs through save, reload, host reopen, duplicate delivery, interruption, and source correction. No wall-clock seed, process identity, random GUID, locale, or hash iteration order may affect the result. Record event order and final state hash.


### 26. Event and system wiring

Name the publisher, consumer, freshness, consequence owner, and delayed callback for every event. The preferred order is upstream world or owner fact, canonical result, projection, narrative callback, then UI refresh. Duplicate events are ignored or folded at the owner boundary. For Shelter Automation and Power Recovery, cross-system callbacks may touch power, water, air, warmth, building, schedule, duty, needs, accessibility, and maintenance, but each callback must remain read-only until it asks the correct owner to mutate.


### 27. Godot host route

The host route exposes one cold-room demand spike resolved through a current owner event with reserve explanation and accessible warning through the existing provider and panel lifecycle. It defines route registration, focus order, keyboard/controller bindings, refresh signal, disposal, close/back behavior, loading, empty, stale, blocked, redacted, complete, and error states. The panel does not count, reserve, score, or simulate. Optional visuals or audio degrade to text while preserving the command and a reasoned next action.


### 28. UI, UX, and accessibility

Accessibility acceptance for Shelter Automation and Power Recovery includes readable text, scaling, contrast, non-color cues, reduced motion, sound-off alternatives, controller parity, focus retention, pagination, cancellation, and safe long strings. Uncertainty from reserve margin, room demand, warmth age, duty coverage, water pressure, and recovery time is stated in text with source age. Redaction or consent blocks explain why content is unavailable. Refresh and disposal are idempotent, and a failed provider leaves the player with a recoverable state.


### 29. Narrative and side-quest integration

The content layer uses cold rooms, repair shifts, rationed heat, duty conflicts, accessible warnings, and reserve callbacks as grounded evidence. The representative branch has discovery, interpretation, complication, competing interest, escalation, irreversible choice, quiet outcome, costly outcome, refusal outcome, unresolved outcome, delayed callback, repeat policy, retirement rule, and correction behavior. Characters and locations use canonical IDs. Narrative text may reveal an observed owner fact but cannot create one.


### 30. Failure-mode attack

Attack Shelter Automation and Power Recovery with null state, empty catalog, missing ID, duplicate ID, out-of-range value, stale source, corrected content, zero resources, dead actor, inaccessible route, completed quest, repeated event, huge value, negative value, unexpected event order, host reload, save during transition, corrupted save, deterministic replay, and unmounted UI. For each case, state whether the owner rejects, defaults, preserves old evidence, releases a reservation, or exposes a visible blocked state.


### 31. Performance and diagnostics

Set a bounded budget for Shelter Automation and Power Recovery: first-page latency, refresh allocations, event fan-out, save delta, worst-case records, and cancellation. High-volume records are paged or lazy-loaded. Caches have one owner and invalidation. Diagnostics report source, consumer, age, and reason without storing private free text. A budget failure degrades to stale or empty presentation and never mutates authoritative state.


### 32. Focused test strategy

The focused strategy includes Core happy path, boundary, failure, invariant, event, and deterministic tests; save round-trip, deep-copy, old-version migration, invalid-version rejection, and checksum cases; data schema, duplicate-ID, reference, range, and consumer reachability checks; Core-to-provider and provider-to-Godot route checks; and a bounded headless check if the runtime path is affected. The first test file runs alone before aggregation.


### 33. Phase 0 premise audit

Phase 0 verifies the current premise for Shelter Automation and Power Recovery. Inspect power grid, water, air, warmth, building, schedule, duty, needs, accessibility, and maintenance owners, current data files, routes, save sections, tests, and live claims. Record exact paths and stop if a required seam is missing or claimed by another package. Do not author production code, speculative data, or new tests during this phase unless the current foreman assigns them.


### 34. Phase 1 Core contract

Phase 1 adds the smallest Core command, query, DTO, or event needed for one cold-room demand spike resolved through a current owner event with reserve explanation and accessible warning. It preserves engine independence and existing owner boundaries. The phase completes when invariants, duplicate handling, error results, and state transitions are covered. It must not add a new global registry, parallel ledger, or Godot dependency.


### 35. Phase 2 data and persistence

Phase 2 adds one validator-backed sample for cold rooms, repair shifts, rationed heat, duty conflicts, accessible warnings, and reserve callbacks, then proves migration or stateless reconstruction. Every field is classified as authored, derived, or durable. Save capture and restore are written before any claim of persistence. The phase must not widen the catalog or rename shared IDs until the sample is reachable and replay-stable.


### 36. Phase 3 host and route

Phase 3 binds the Core result to a thin host route. It preserves route registration, focus, close/back, refresh, disposal, error, stale, and empty behavior. The phase completes when a player can perform one cold-room demand spike resolved through a current owner event with reserve explanation and accessible warning and see an observable owner result. It must not place domain calculations in the panel or create a local cache.


### 37. Phase 4 content and callbacks

Phase 4 adds one location or room, one named person or crew, one disturbance, and four outcome branches over the proven route. It connects to journal, archive, relationship, roster, route, shelter, or expedition facts only through current owners. It must not enable a content bank that has no runtime consumer.


### 38. File impact map

Likely areas are Assets/Ashfall.Core for owner contracts, Assets/StreamingAssets/Data for the representative row, src for provider and panel binding, and Ashfall.Core.Tests for focused contracts. Each path remains READ ONLY during premise audit and becomes MODIFY only after an exact claim is accepted. Generated outputs are changed only through their owning generator. No Unity-era path is a target.


### 39. Out of scope

Out of scope for Shelter Automation and Power Recovery: a new global simulation, a second ledger, broad architecture cleanup, unrequested localization sweep, unrelated balance changes, retired Unity behavior, speculative save sections, private telemetry, and a global content browser. Nearby gaps remain visible in the blocker receipt and are not silently bundled.


### 40. Rollback and recovery

Rollback for Shelter Automation and Power Recovery removes or disables the new adapter and content row while preserving old saves. A migration failure rejects the malformed envelope without partial mutation. A host failure unbinds the route while Core remains isolated. A content correction appends evidence. The smallest reversible package is one owner seam, one data row, one host route, one focused test set, and one handoff.


### 41. Definition of done

The slice is ready for implementation handoff when owner, consumer, data, state, save or stateless proof, deterministic replay, host route, accessibility, performance, content callback, focused verification, rollback, and exact file claims all have receipts. The package remains READY FOR PREMISE AUDIT until current evidence confirms the first gate. Word count and prose density never promote it.


### 42. Implementation handoff

The handoff for Shelter Automation and Power Recovery names the objective, current reality, required delta, owner, data row, command, route, save impact, replay fixture, accessibility case, performance budget, content IDs, rollback, open blockers, and next bounded action. MUST PRESERVE: one owner, engine-free Core, JSON authority, deterministic behavior, current save ownership, truthful UI, and current ledger claims. MUST NOT DO: duplicate state, revive retired architecture, or claim integration from compile success alone.

## Plan 8 — Exploration Cartography and Archive — closeout dossier


**Program band:** cartography, expedition, installation, fragment, codex, journal, archive, and provenance
**Primary owners:** cartography, expedition, installation, fragment, codex, journal, archive, and provenance owners
**Required delta:** a provenance-aware survey projection over one existing observation, with deterministic revision, uncertainty, and archive custody
**First safe slice:** one survey mark corrected after a return trip and cited in an existing archive surface
**Material anchors:** a survey pin, a weathered compass card, a fragment sleeve, and a map margin note
**Observable signals:** coordinate confidence, instrument condition, provenance age, archive custody, route risk, and correction state
**Content family:** survey crews, corrected maps, lost instruments, archive disputes, return trips, and evidence callbacks
**Primary risks:** second map, fragment inventory duplication, non-deterministic ordering, false certainty, and archive overwrite

This dossier closes the planning surface for Plan 8 without claiming runtime integration. Every subsection is a handoff requirement, not an implementation result.

### 15. Closeout objective

Close Exploration Cartography and Archive as a premise-gated package. The objective is to turn a provenance-aware survey projection over one existing observation, with deterministic revision, uncertainty, and archive custody into one reviewable vertical slice while preserving cartography, expedition, installation, fragment, codex, journal, archive, and provenance owners. The package is successful when the first slice has a named publisher, a consumer that can be reached by a current route, a reversible command, truthful feedback, and a receipt that states what remains unowned.


### 16. Existing authority inventory

The authority inventory for Exploration Cartography and Archive is the current set of cartography, expedition, installation, fragment, codex, journal, archive, and provenance. The plan may reference a survey pin, a weathered compass card, a fragment sleeve, and a map margin note and coordinate confidence, instrument condition, provenance age, archive custody, route risk, and correction state, but those observations become gameplay facts only when an existing owner emits them. Any older plan text that implies a new ledger, registry, or simulation is treated as a hypothesis until a current source path proves it.


### 17. Required delta

## Plan 9 — Food Preservation, Nutrition, and Kitchen Resilience — closeout dossier


**Program band:** inventory, crafting, needs, disease, shelter, greenhouse, economy, trade, and kitchen content
**Primary owners:** inventory, crafting, needs, disease, shelter, greenhouse, economy, and trade owners
**Required delta:** a preservation and nutrition projection over existing inventory, needs, spoilage, and kitchen owners, with no second food or calorie ledger
**First safe slice:** one validated preservation batch with source age, nutrition consequence, refusal path, and recovery callback
**Material anchors:** a crock seal, a measured portion card, a greenhouse basket, and a pantry repair note
**Observable signals:** batch age, seal confidence, nutrient balance, appetite drift, fuel cost, and recovery route
**Content family:** pantry disputes, preservation lessons, greenhouse failures, meal promises, ration choices, and repair callbacks
**Primary risks:** duplicate spoilage state, hidden nutrition math, inventory mutation in UI, and stale batch display

This dossier closes the planning surface for Plan 9 without claiming runtime integration. Every subsection is a handoff requirement, not an implementation result.

### 15. Closeout objective

Close Food Preservation, Nutrition, and Kitchen Resilience as a premise-gated package. The objective is to turn a preservation and nutrition projection over existing inventory, needs, spoilage, and kitchen owners, with no second food or calorie ledger into one reviewable vertical slice while preserving inventory, crafting, needs, disease, shelter, greenhouse, economy, and trade owners. The package is successful when the first slice has a named publisher, a consumer that can be reached by a current route, a reversible command, truthful feedback, and a receipt that states what remains unowned.


### 16. Existing authority inventory

The authority inventory for Food Preservation, Nutrition, and Kitchen Resilience is the current set of inventory, crafting, needs, disease, shelter, greenhouse, economy, trade, and kitchen content. Inspect each owner in source, data, save, host, and tests. The plan may reference a crock seal, a measured portion card, a greenhouse basket, and a pantry repair note and batch age, seal confidence, nutrient balance, appetite drift, fuel cost, and recovery route, but those observations become gameplay facts only when an existing owner emits them. Any older plan text that implies a new ledger, registry, or simulation is treated as a hypothesis until a current source path proves it.


### 17. Required delta

The smallest required delta is a preservation and nutrition projection over existing inventory, needs, spoilage, and kitchen owners, with no second food or calorie ledger. The delta does not include a new framework, a new global manager, or broad refactoring. It is one command or read projection, one representative row, one observable outcome, and one recovery path. The builder records the old behavior, the missing behavior, and the exact consumer that will make the change visible.


### 18. Premise and collision receipt

The premise receipt searches for alternate names, partial producers, retired implementations, stale IDs, test-only shims, and existing UI routes. Collision review is especially important for duplicate spoilage state, hidden nutrition math, inventory mutation in UI, and stale batch display. A collision is resolved by extending the current owner, adapting a current provider, or marking the proposal blocked. The receipt must state what was searched and which result is current.


### 19. Single-owner assignment

Assign simulation rules to Core, authored facts to JSON, persistence to the current save owner, presentation to Godot, input to the route, and validation to existing integrity gates. For Food Preservation, Nutrition, and Kitchen Resilience, the primary owner families are inventory, crafting, needs, disease, shelter, greenhouse, economy, and trade owners. If a value appears in more than one plan, the shared seam carries a canonical ID, source age, confidence, and event sequence instead of copied mutable state.


### 20. First safe player loop

The first safe loop for Food Preservation, Nutrition, and Kitchen Resilience is notice, inspect, compare, choose, observe, and recover. The proposed slice is one validated preservation batch with source age, nutrition consequence, refusal path, and recovery callback. The player can verify, accept, defer, share, delegate, refuse, or return. Quiet success protects a routine, costly success spends a real resource, refusal preserves the earlier state, and unresolved play leaves a visible unknown. No timer, color, or sound cue is the sole proof of outcome.


### 21. Data contract

The representative data contract describes pantry disputes, preservation lessons, greenhouse failures, meal promises, ration choices, and repair callbacks using stable snake_case IDs, schema_version, lifecycle, source, localization keys, references, bounded values, and correction notes. It distinguishes authored configuration, derived projection, and durable state. Duplicate IDs, missing references, unsupported fields, empty consumers, corrected rows, and old versions have explicit outcomes. A sample row must connect a crock seal, a measured portion card, a greenhouse basket, and a pantry repair note to an owner fact without turning the anchor into a second authority.


### 22. State ladder

The state ladder for Food Preservation, Nutrition, and Kitchen Resilience covers offered, inspected, accepted, active, paused, completed, failed, expired, corrected, and replay states only where the slice needs them. Every transition names the mutator, reservation, release rule, event, receipt, and interruption behavior. A repeated command is idempotent or refused. A correction adds evidence and preserves prior history. The ladder must make batch age, seal confidence, nutrient balance, appetite drift, fuel cost, and recovery route observable without storing duplicate counters.


### 23. Data flow

The data flow is INPUT → VALIDATION → CORE STATE or READ MODEL → DOMAIN EVENT → HOST PROVIDER → UI → PLAYER ACTION → COMMAND → CORE MUTATION → SAVE. For Food Preservation, Nutrition, and Kitchen Resilience, one validated preservation batch with source age, nutrition consequence, refusal path, and recovery callback is the sample path. The flow identifies source age, confidence, stale behavior, and the delayed callback. Subscribers consume facts and request owner mutations; they never reach into foreign ledgers.


### 24. Save and migration

Capture only durable owner facts needed by Food Preservation, Nutrition, and Kitchen Resilience. Define CaptureState, RestoreState, version, old-save defaults, malformed-envelope refusal, checksum behavior, null and empty semantics, and deep-copy isolation. If the slice is a pure projection, say so and prove reconstruction after reload. A save during interruption must not duplicate rewards, penalties, messages, treatment, route results, archive entries, or relationship changes.


### 25. Determinism and replay

Use the seeded RNG contract only if the slice needs chance. Otherwise keep the result pure. Stable ordering for batch age, seal confidence, nutrient balance, appetite drift, fuel cost, and recovery route uses canonical IDs and authored ordinals. Pair same-seed inputs through save, reload, host reopen, duplicate delivery, interruption, and source correction. No wall-clock seed, process identity, random GUID, locale, or hash iteration order may affect the result. Record event order and final state hash.


### 26. Event and system wiring

Name the publisher, consumer, freshness, consequence owner, and delayed callback for every event. The preferred order is upstream world or owner fact, canonical result, projection, narrative callback, then UI refresh. Duplicate events are ignored or folded at the owner boundary. For Food Preservation, Nutrition, and Kitchen Resilience, cross-system callbacks may touch inventory, crafting, needs, disease, shelter, greenhouse, economy, trade, and kitchen content, but each callback must remain read-only until it asks the correct owner to mutate.


### 27. Godot host route

The host route exposes one validated preservation batch with source age, nutrition consequence, refusal path, and recovery callback through the existing provider and panel lifecycle. It defines route registration, focus order, keyboard/controller bindings, refresh signal, disposal, close/back behavior, loading, empty, stale, blocked, redacted, complete, and error states. The panel does not count, reserve, score, or simulate. Optional visuals or audio degrade to text while preserving the command and a reasoned next action.


### 28. UI, UX, and accessibility

Accessibility acceptance for Food Preservation, Nutrition, and Kitchen Resilience includes readable text, scaling, contrast, non-color cues, reduced motion, sound-off alternatives, controller parity, focus retention, pagination, cancellation, and safe long strings. Uncertainty from batch age, seal confidence, nutrient balance, appetite drift, fuel cost, and recovery route is stated in text with source age. Redaction or consent blocks explain why content is unavailable. Refresh and disposal are idempotent, and a failed provider leaves the player with a recoverable state.


### 29. Narrative and side-quest integration

The content layer uses pantry disputes, preservation lessons, greenhouse failures, meal promises, ration choices, and repair callbacks as grounded evidence. The representative branch has discovery, interpretation, complication, competing interest, escalation, irreversible choice, quiet outcome, costly outcome, refusal outcome, unresolved outcome, delayed callback, repeat policy, retirement rule, and correction behavior. Characters and locations use canonical IDs. Narrative text may reveal an observed owner fact but cannot create one.


### 30. Failure-mode attack

Attack Food Preservation, Nutrition, and Kitchen Resilience with null state, empty catalog, missing ID, duplicate ID, out-of-range value, stale source, corrected content, zero resources, dead actor, inaccessible route, completed quest, repeated event, huge value, negative value, unexpected event order, host reload, save during transition, corrupted save, deterministic replay, and unmounted UI. For each case, state whether the owner rejects, defaults, preserves old evidence, releases a reservation, or exposes a visible blocked state.


### 31. Performance and diagnostics

Set a bounded budget for Food Preservation, Nutrition, and Kitchen Resilience: first-page latency, refresh allocations, event fan-out, save delta, worst-case records, and cancellation. High-volume records are paged or lazy-loaded. Caches have one owner and invalidation. Diagnostics report source, consumer, age, and reason without storing private free text. A budget failure degrades to stale or empty presentation and never mutates authoritative state.


### 32. Focused test strategy

The focused strategy includes Core happy path, boundary, failure, invariant, event, and deterministic tests; save round-trip, deep-copy, old-version migration, invalid-version rejection, and checksum cases; data schema, duplicate-ID, reference, range, and consumer reachability checks; Core-to-provider and provider-to-Godot route checks; and a bounded headless check if the runtime path is affected. The first test file runs alone before aggregation.


### 33. Phase 0 premise audit

Phase 0 verifies the current premise for Food Preservation, Nutrition, and Kitchen Resilience. Inspect inventory, crafting, needs, disease, shelter, greenhouse, economy, and trade owners, current data files, routes, save sections, tests, and live claims. Record exact paths and stop if a required seam is missing or claimed by another package. Do not author production code, speculative data, or new tests during this phase unless the current foreman assigns them.


### 34. Phase 1 Core contract

Phase 1 adds the smallest Core command, query, DTO, or event needed for one validated preservation batch with source age, nutrition consequence, refusal path, and recovery callback. It preserves engine independence and existing owner boundaries. The phase completes when invariants, duplicate handling, error results, and state transitions are covered. It must not add a new global registry, parallel ledger, or Godot dependency.


### 35. Phase 2 data and persistence

Phase 2 adds one validator-backed sample for pantry disputes, preservation lessons, greenhouse failures, meal promises, ration choices, and repair callbacks, then proves migration or stateless reconstruction. Every field is classified as authored, derived, or durable. Save capture and restore are written before any claim of persistence. The phase must not widen the catalog or rename shared IDs until the sample is reachable and replay-stable.


### 36. Phase 3 host and route

Phase 3 binds the Core result to a thin host route. It preserves route registration, focus, close/back, refresh, disposal, error, stale, and empty behavior. The phase completes when a player can perform one validated preservation batch with source age, nutrition consequence, refusal path, and recovery callback and see an observable owner result. It must not place domain calculations in the panel or create a local cache.


### 37. Phase 4 content and callbacks

Phase 4 adds one location or room, one named person or crew, one disturbance, and four outcome branches over the proven route. It connects to journal, archive, relationship, roster, route, shelter, or expedition facts only through current owners. It must not enable a content bank that has no runtime consumer.


### 38. File impact map

Likely areas are Assets/Ashfall.Core for owner contracts, Assets/StreamingAssets/Data for the representative row, src for provider and panel binding, and Ashfall.Core.Tests for focused contracts. Each path remains READ ONLY during premise audit and becomes MODIFY only after an exact claim is accepted. Generated outputs are changed only through their owning generator. No Unity-era path is a target.


### 39. Out of scope

Out of scope for Food Preservation, Nutrition, and Kitchen Resilience: a new global simulation, a second ledger, broad architecture cleanup, unrequested localization sweep, unrelated balance changes, retired Unity behavior, speculative save sections, private telemetry, and a global content browser. Nearby gaps remain visible in the blocker receipt and are not silently bundled.


### 40. Rollback and recovery

Rollback for Food Preservation, Nutrition, and Kitchen Resilience removes or disables the new adapter and content row while preserving old saves. A migration failure rejects the malformed envelope without partial mutation. A host failure unbinds the route while Core remains isolated. A content correction appends evidence. The smallest reversible package is one owner seam, one data row, one host route, one focused test set, and one handoff.

The slice is ready for implementation handoff when owner, consumer, data, state, save or stateless proof, deterministic replay, host route, accessibility, performance, content callback, focused verification, rollback, and exact file claims all have receipts. ## Plan 10 — Perimeter Watch, Threat Signals, and Safe Response — closeout dossier


**Program band:** security, combat, wildlife, weather, expedition, radio, roster, shelter, and watch duty
**Primary owners:** security, combat, wildlife, weather, expedition, radio, roster, shelter, and watch-duty owners
**Required delta:** a safe-response signal projection over existing observations and duty facts, with no second threat, alert, or combat authority
**First safe slice:** one corroborated perimeter observation with uncertainty, acknowledge/defer/refuse actions, and a safe return callback
**Material anchors:** a signal marker, a watch cup, a route shutter, and a roster handoff card
**Observable signals:** source confidence, alert age, fatigue burden, route reach, weather interference, and return state
**Content family:** watch rotations, false alarms, wildlife traces, weather misreads, radio calls, and safe-return callbacks
**Primary risks:** alert spam, combat mutation in UI, fatigue duplication, threat certainty inflation, and inaccessible warnings

This dossier closes the planning surface for Plan 10 without claiming runtime integration. Every subsection is a handoff requirement, not an implementation result.

### 15. Closeout objective

Close Perimeter Watch, Threat Signals, and Safe Response as a premise-gated package. The objective is to turn a safe-response signal projection over existing observations and duty facts, with no second threat, alert, or combat authority into one reviewable vertical slice while preserving security, combat, wildlife, weather, expedition, radio, roster, shelter, and watch-duty owners. The package is successful when the first slice has a named publisher, a consumer that can be reached by a current route, a reversible command, truthful feedback, and a receipt that states what remains unowned.


### 16. Existing authority inventory

The authority inventory for Perimeter Watch, Threat Signals, and Safe Response is the current set of security, combat, wildlife, weather, expedition, radio, roster, shelter, and watch duty. Inspect each owner in source, data, save, host, and tests. The plan may reference a signal marker, a watch cup, a route shutter, and a roster handoff card and source confidence, alert age, fatigue burden, route reach, weather interference, and return state, but those observations become gameplay facts only when an existing owner emits them. Any older plan text that implies a new ledger, registry, or simulation is treated as a hypothesis until a current source path proves it.


### 17. Required delta

The smallest required delta is a safe-response signal projection over existing observations and duty facts, with no second threat, alert, or combat authority. The delta does not include a new framework, a new global manager, or broad refactoring. It is one command or read projection, one representative row, one observable outcome, and one recovery path. The builder records the old behavior, the missing behavior, and the exact consumer that will make the change visible.


### 18. Premise and collision receipt

The premise receipt searches for alternate names, partial producers, retired implementations, stale IDs, test-only shims, and existing UI routes. Collision review is especially important for alert spam, combat mutation in UI, fatigue duplication, threat certainty inflation, and inaccessible warnings. A collision is resolved by extending the current owner, adapting a current provider, or marking the proposal blocked. The receipt must state what was searched and which result is current.


### 19. Single-owner assignment

Assign simulation rules to Core, authored facts to JSON, persistence to the current save owner, presentation to Godot, input to the route, and validation to existing integrity gates. For Perimeter Watch, Threat Signals, and Safe Response, the primary owner families are security, combat, wildlife, weather, expedition, radio, roster, shelter, and watch-duty owners. If a value appears in more than one plan, the shared seam carries a canonical ID, source age, confidence, and event sequence instead of copied mutable state.


### 20. First safe player loop

The first safe loop for Perimeter Watch, Threat Signals, and Safe Response is notice, inspect, compare, choose, observe, and recover. The proposed slice is one corroborated perimeter observation with uncertainty, acknowledge/defer/refuse actions, and a safe return callback. The player can verify, accept, defer, share, delegate, refuse, or return. Quiet success protects a routine, costly success spends a real resource, refusal preserves the earlier state, and unresolved play leaves a visible unknown. No timer, color, or sound cue is the sole proof of outcome.


### 21. Data contract

The representative data contract describes watch rotations, false alarms, wildlife traces, weather misreads, radio calls, and safe-return callbacks using stable snake_case IDs, schema_version, lifecycle, source, localization keys, references, bounded values, and correction notes. It distinguishes authored configuration, derived projection, and durable state. Duplicate IDs, missing references, unsupported fields, empty consumers, corrected rows, and old versions have explicit outcomes. A sample row must connect a signal marker, a watch cup, a route shutter, and a roster handoff card to an owner fact without turning the anchor into a second authority.


### 22. State ladder

The state ladder for Perimeter Watch, Threat Signals, and Safe Response covers offered, inspected, accepted, active, paused, completed, failed, expired, corrected, and replay states only where the slice needs them. Every transition names the mutator, reservation, release rule, event, receipt, and interruption behavior. A repeated command is idempotent or refused. A correction adds evidence and preserves prior history. The ladder must make source confidence, alert age, fatigue burden, route reach, weather interference, and return state observable without storing duplicate counters.


### 23. Data flow

The data flow is INPUT → VALIDATION → CORE STATE or READ MODEL → DOMAIN EVENT → HOST PROVIDER → UI → PLAYER ACTION → COMMAND → CORE MUTATION → SAVE. For Perimeter Watch, Threat Signals, and Safe Response, one corroborated perimeter observation with uncertainty, acknowledge/defer/refuse actions, and a safe return callback is the sample path. The flow identifies source age, confidence, stale behavior, and the delayed callback. Subscribers consume facts and request owner mutations; they never reach into foreign ledgers.


### 24. Save and migration

Capture only durable owner facts needed by Perimeter Watch, Threat Signals, and Safe Response. Define CaptureState, RestoreState, version, old-save defaults, malformed-envelope refusal, checksum behavior, null and empty semantics, and deep-copy isolation. If the slice is a pure projection, say so and prove reconstruction after reload. A save during interruption must not duplicate rewards, penalties, messages, treatment, route results, archive entries, or relationship changes.


### 25. Determinism and replay

Use the seeded RNG contract only if the slice needs chance. Otherwise keep the result pure. Stable ordering for source confidence, alert age, fatigue burden, route reach, weather interference, and return state uses canonical IDs and authored ordinals. Pair same-seed inputs through save, reload, host reopen, duplicate delivery, interruption, and source correction. No wall-clock seed, process identity, random GUID, locale, or hash iteration order may affect the result. Record event order and final state hash.


### 26. Event and system wiring

Name the publisher, consumer, freshness, consequence owner, and delayed callback for every event. The preferred order is upstream world or owner fact, canonical result, projection, narrative callback, then UI refresh. Duplicate events are ignored or folded at the owner boundary. For Perimeter Watch, Threat Signals, and Safe Response, cross-system callbacks may touch security, combat, wildlife, weather, expedition, radio, roster, shelter, and watch duty, but each callback must remain read-only until it asks the correct owner to mutate.


### 27. Godot host route

The host route exposes one corroborated perimeter observation with uncertainty, acknowledge/defer/refuse actions, and a safe return callback through the existing provider and panel lifecycle. It defines route registration, focus order, keyboard/controller bindings, refresh signal, disposal, close/back behavior, loading, empty, stale, blocked, redacted, complete, and error states. The panel does not count, reserve, score, or simulate. Optional visuals or audio degrade to text while preserving the command and a reasoned next action.


### 28. UI, UX, and accessibility

Accessibility acceptance for Perimeter Watch, Threat Signals, and Safe Response includes readable text, scaling, contrast, non-color cues, reduced motion, sound-off alternatives, controller parity, focus retention, pagination, cancellation, and safe long strings. Uncertainty from source confidence, alert age, fatigue burden, route reach, weather interference, and return state is stated in text with source age. Redaction or consent blocks explain why content is unavailable. Refresh and disposal are idempotent, and a failed provider leaves the player with a recoverable state.


### 29. Narrative and side-quest integration

The content layer uses watch rotations, false alarms, wildlife traces, weather misreads, radio calls, and safe-return callbacks as grounded evidence. The representative branch has discovery, interpretation, complication, competing interest, escalation, irreversible choice, quiet outcome, costly outcome, refusal outcome, unresolved outcome, delayed callback, repeat policy, retirement rule, and correction behavior. Characters and locations use canonical IDs. Narrative text may reveal an observed owner fact but cannot create one.


### 30. Failure-mode attack

Attack Perimeter Watch, Threat Signals, and Safe Response with null state, empty catalog, missing ID, duplicate ID, out-of-range value, stale source, corrected content, zero resources, dead actor, inaccessible route, completed quest, repeated event, huge value, negative value, unexpected event order, host reload, save during transition, corrupted save, deterministic replay, and unmounted UI. For each case, state whether the owner rejects, defaults, preserves old evidence, releases a reservation, or exposes a visible blocked state.


### 31. Performance and diagnostics

Set a bounded budget for Perimeter Watch, Threat Signals, and Safe Response: first-page latency, refresh allocations, event fan-out, save delta, worst-case records, and cancellation. High-volume records are paged or lazy-loaded. Caches have one owner and invalidation. Diagnostics report source, consumer, age, and reason without storing private free text. A budget failure degrades to stale or empty presentation and never mutates authoritative state.


### 32. Focused test strategy

The focused strategy includes Core happy path, boundary, failure, invariant, event, and deterministic tests; save round-trip, deep-copy, old-version migration, invalid-version rejection, and checksum cases; data schema, duplicate-ID, reference, range, and consumer reachability checks; Core-to-provider and provider-to-Godot route checks; and a bounded headless check if the runtime path is affected. The first test file runs alone before aggregation.


### 33. Phase 0 premise audit

Phase 0 verifies the current premise for Perimeter Watch, Threat Signals, and Safe Response. Inspect security, combat, wildlife, weather, expedition, radio, roster, shelter, and watch-duty owners, current data files, routes, save sections, tests, and live claims. Record exact paths and stop if a required seam is missing or claimed by another package. Do not author production code, speculative data, or new tests during this phase unless the current foreman assigns them.


### 34. Phase 1 Core contract

Phase 1 adds the smallest Core command, query, DTO, or event needed for one corroborated perimeter observation with uncertainty, acknowledge/defer/refuse actions, and a safe return callback. It preserves engine independence and existing owner boundaries. The phase completes when invariants, duplicate handling, error results, and state transitions are covered. It must not add a new global registry, parallel ledger, or Godot dependency.


### 35. Phase 2 data and persistence

Phase 2 adds one validator-backed sample for watch rotations, false alarms, wildlife traces, weather misreads, radio calls, and safe-return callbacks, then proves migration or stateless reconstruction. Every field is classified as authored, derived, or durable. Save capture and restore are written before any claim of persistence. The phase must not widen the catalog or rename shared IDs until the sample is reachable and replay-stable.


### 36. Phase 3 host and route

Phase 3 binds the Core result to a thin host route. It preserves route registration, focus, close/back, refresh, disposal, error, stale, and empty behavior. The phase completes when a player can perform one corroborated perimeter observation with uncertainty, acknowledge/defer/refuse actions, and a safe return callback and see an observable owner result. It must not place domain calculations in the panel or create a local cache.


### 37. Phase 4 content and callbacks

Phase 4 adds one location or room, one named person or crew, one disturbance, and four outcome branches over the proven route. It connects to journal, archive, relationship, roster, route, shelter, or expedition facts only through current owners. It must not enable a content bank that has no runtime consumer.


### 38. File impact map

Likely areas are Assets/Ashfall.Core for owner contracts, Assets/StreamingAssets/Data for the representative row, src for provider and panel binding, and Ashfall.Core.Tests for focused contracts. Each path remains READ ONLY during premise audit and becomes MODIFY only after an exact claim is accepted. Generated outputs are changed only through their owning generator. No Unity-era path is a target.


### 39. Out of scope

Out of scope for Perimeter Watch, Threat Signals, and Safe Response: a new global simulation, a second ledger, broad architecture cleanup, unrequested localization sweep, unrelated balance changes, retired Unity behavior, speculative save sections, private telemetry, and a global content browser. Nearby gaps remain visible in the blocker receipt and are not silently bundled.


### 40. Rollback and recovery

Rollback for Perimeter Watch, Threat Signals, and Safe Response removes or disables the new adapter and content row while preserving old saves. A migration failure rejects the malformed envelope without partial mutation. A host failure unbinds the route while Core remains isolated. A content correction appends evidence. The smallest reversible package is one owner seam, one data row, one host route, one focused test set, and one handoff.


### 41. Definition of done

The slice is ready for implementation handoff when owner, consumer, data, state, save or stateless proof, deterministic replay, host route, accessibility, performance, content callback, focused verification, rollback, and exact file claims all have receipts. The package remains READY FOR PREMISE AUDIT until current evidence confirms the first gate. Word count and prose density never promote it.


### 42. Implementation handoff

The handoff for Perimeter Watch, Threat Signals, and Safe Response names the objective, current reality, required delta, owner, data row, command, route, save impact, replay fixture, accessibility case, performance budget, content IDs, rollback, open blockers, and next bounded action. MUST PRESERVE: one owner, engine-free Core, JSON authority, deterministic behavior, current save ownership, truthful UI, and current ledger claims. MUST NOT DO: duplicate state, revive retired architecture, or claim integration from compile success alone.

The proposed slice is one tool checkout and return with condition evidence, learner acknowledgement, failure recovery, and archive callback. The player can verify, accept, defer, share, delegate, refuse, or return. Quiet success protects a routine, costly success spends a real resource, refusal preserves the earlier state, and unresolved play leaves a visible unknown. No timer, color, or sound cue is the sole proof of outcome.


### 21. Data contract

The representative data contract describes salvage lessons, apprentice errors, tool disputes, parts shortages, repair milestones, and workshop callbacks using stable snake_case IDs, schema_version, lifecycle, source, localization keys, references, bounded values, and correction notes. It distinguishes authored configuration, derived projection, and durable state. Duplicate IDs, missing references, unsupported fields, empty consumers, corrected rows, and old versions have explicit outcomes. A sample row must connect a condition tag, a repair bench card, a parts tray, and a learner mark to an owner fact without turning the anchor into a second authority.


### 22. State ladder

The state ladder for Tool Libraries, Salvage, and Repair Apprenticeships covers offered, inspected, accepted, active, paused, completed, failed, expired, corrected, and replay states only where the slice needs them. Every transition names the mutator, reservation, release rule, event, receipt, and interruption behavior. A repeated command is idempotent or refused. A correction adds evidence and preserves prior history. The ladder must make condition age, part count, queue debt, learner confidence, reservation status, and return route observable without storing duplicate counters.


### 23. Data flow

The data flow is INPUT → VALIDATION → CORE STATE or READ MODEL → DOMAIN EVENT → HOST PROVIDER → UI → PLAYER ACTION → COMMAND → CORE MUTATION → SAVE. For Tool Libraries, Salvage, and Repair Apprenticeships, one tool checkout and return with condition evidence, learner acknowledgement, failure recovery, and archive callback is the sample path. The flow identifies source age, confidence, stale behavior, and the delayed callback. Subscribers consume facts and request owner mutations; they never reach into foreign ledgers.


### 24. Save and migration

Capture only durable owner facts needed by Tool Libraries, Salvage, and Repair Apprenticeships. Define CaptureState, RestoreState, version, old-save defaults, malformed-envelope refusal, checksum behavior, null and empty semantics, and deep-copy isolation. If the slice is a pure projection, say so and prove reconstruction after reload. A save during interruption must not duplicate rewards, penalties, messages, treatment, route results, archive entries, or relationship changes.


### 25. Determinism and replay

Use the seeded RNG contract only if the slice needs chance. Otherwise keep the result pure. Stable ordering for condition age, part count, queue debt, learner confidence, reservation status, and return route uses canonical IDs and authored ordinals. Pair same-seed inputs through save, reload, host reopen, duplicate delivery, interruption, and source correction. No wall-clock seed, process identity, random GUID, locale, or hash iteration order may affect the result. Record event order and final state hash.


### 26. Event and system wiring

Name the publisher, consumer, freshness, consequence owner, and delayed callback for every event. The preferred order is upstream world or owner fact, canonical result, projection, narrative callback, then UI refresh. Duplicate events are ignored or folded at the owner boundary. For Tool Libraries, Salvage, and Repair Apprenticeships, cross-system callbacks may touch inventory, crafting, condition, work-order, duty, economy, expedition, shelter workshop, and apprenticeship, but each callback must remain read-only until it asks the correct owner to mutate.


### 27. Godot host route

The host route exposes one tool checkout and return with condition evidence, learner acknowledgement, failure recovery, and archive callback through the existing provider and panel lifecycle. It defines route registration, focus order, keyboard/controller bindings, refresh signal, disposal, close/back behavior, loading, empty, stale, blocked, redacted, complete, and error states. The panel does not count, reserve, score, or simulate. Optional visuals or audio degrade to text while preserving the command and a reasoned next action.


### 28. UI, UX, and accessibility

Accessibility acceptance for Tool Libraries, Salvage, and Repair Apprenticeships includes readable text, scaling, contrast, non-color cues, reduced motion, sound-off alternatives, controller parity, focus retention, pagination, cancellation, and safe long strings. Uncertainty from condition age, part count, queue debt, learner confidence, reservation status, and return route is stated in text with source age. Redaction or consent blocks explain why content is unavailable. Refresh and disposal are idempotent, and a failed provider leaves the player with a recoverable state.


### 29. Narrative and side-quest integration

The content layer uses salvage lessons, apprentice errors, tool disputes, parts shortages, repair milestones, and workshop callbacks as grounded evidence. The representative branch has discovery, interpretation, complication, competing interest, escalation, irreversible choice, quiet outcome, costly outcome, refusal outcome, unresolved outcome, delayed callback, repeat policy, retirement rule, and correction behavior. Characters and locations use canonical IDs. Narrative text may reveal an observed owner fact but cannot create one.


### 30. Failure-mode attack

Attack Tool Libraries, Salvage, and Repair Apprenticeships with null state, empty catalog, missing ID, duplicate ID, out-of-range value, stale source, corrected content, zero resources, dead actor, inaccessible route, completed quest, repeated event, huge value, negative value, unexpected event order, host reload, save during transition, corrupted save, deterministic replay, and unmounted UI. For each case, state whether the owner rejects, defaults, preserves old evidence, releases a reservation, or exposes a visible blocked state.


### 31. Performance and diagnostics

Set a bounded budget for Tool Libraries, Salvage, and Repair Apprenticeships: first-page latency, refresh allocations, event fan-out, save delta, worst-case records, and cancellation. High-volume records are paged or lazy-loaded. Caches have one owner and invalidation. Diagnostics report source, consumer, age, and reason without storing private free text. A budget failure degrades to stale or empty presentation and never mutates authoritative state.


### 32. Focused test strategy

The focused strategy includes Core happy path, boundary, failure, invariant, event, and deterministic tests; save round-trip, deep-copy, old-version migration, invalid-version rejection, and checksum cases; data schema, duplicate-ID, reference, range, and consumer reachability checks; Core-to-provider and provider-to-Godot route checks; and a bounded headless check if the runtime path is affected. The first test file runs alone before aggregation.


### 33. Phase 0 premise audit

Phase 0 verifies the current premise for Tool Libraries, Salvage, and Repair Apprenticeships. Inspect inventory, crafting, condition, work-order, duty, economy, expedition, shelter workshop, and apprenticeship owners, current data files, routes, save sections, tests, and live claims. Record exact paths and stop if a required seam is missing or claimed by another package. Do not author production code, speculative data, or new tests during this phase unless the current foreman assigns them.


### 34. Phase 1 Core contract

Phase 1 adds the smallest Core command, query, DTO, or event needed for one tool checkout and return with condition evidence, learner acknowledgement, failure recovery, and archive callback. It preserves engine independence and existing owner boundaries. The phase completes when invariants, duplicate handling, error results, and state transitions are covered. It must not add a new global registry, parallel ledger, or Godot dependency.


### 35. Phase 2 data and persistence

Phase 2 adds one validator-backed sample for salvage lessons, apprentice errors, tool disputes, parts shortages, repair milestones, and workshop callbacks, then proves migration or stateless reconstruction. Every field is classified as authored, derived, or durable. Save capture and restore are written before any claim of persistence. The phase must not widen the catalog or rename shared IDs until the sample is reachable and replay-stable.


### 36. Phase 3 host and route

Phase 3 binds the Core result to a thin host route. It preserves route registration, focus, close/back, refresh, disposal, error, stale, and empty behavior. The phase completes when a player can perform one tool checkout and return with condition evidence, learner acknowledgement, failure recovery, and archive callback and see an observable owner result. It must not place domain calculations in the panel or create a local cache.


### 37. Phase 4 content and callbacks

Phase 4 adds one location or room, one named person or crew, one disturbance, and four outcome branches over the proven route. It connects to journal, archive, relationship, roster, route, shelter, or expedition facts only through current owners. It must not enable a content bank that has no runtime consumer.


### 38. File impact map

Likely areas are Assets/Ashfall.Core for owner contracts, Assets/StreamingAssets/Data for the representative row, src for provider and panel binding, and Ashfall.Core.Tests for focused contracts. Each path remains READ ONLY during premise audit and becomes MODIFY only after an exact claim is accepted. Generated outputs are changed only through their owning generator. No Unity-era path is a target.


### 39. Out of scope

Out of scope for Tool Libraries, Salvage, and Repair Apprenticeships: a new global simulation, a second ledger, broad architecture cleanup, unrequested localization sweep, unrelated balance changes, retired Unity behavior, speculative save sections, private telemetry, and a global content browser. Nearby gaps remain visible in the blocker receipt and are not silently bundled.


### 40. Rollback and recovery

Rollback for Tool Libraries, Salvage, and Repair Apprenticeships removes or disables the new adapter and content row while preserving old saves. A migration failure rejects the malformed envelope without partial mutation. A host failure unbinds the route while Core remains isolated. A content correction appends evidence. The smallest reversible package is one owner seam, one data row, one host route, one focused test set, and one handoff.


### 41. Definition of done

The slice is ready for implementation handoff when owner, consumer, data, state, save or stateless proof, deterministic replay, host route, accessibility, performance, content callback, focused verification, rollback, and exact file claims all have receipts. The package remains READY FOR PREMISE AUDIT until current evidence confirms the first gate. Word count and prose density never promote it.


### 42. Implementation handoff

The handoff for Tool Libraries, Salvage, and Repair Apprenticeships names the objective, current reality, required delta, owner, data row, command, route, save impact, replay fixture, accessibility case, performance budget, content IDs, rollback, open blockers, and next bounded action. MUST PRESERVE: one owner, engine-free Core, JSON authority, deterministic behavior, current save ownership, truthful UI, and current ledger claims. MUST NOT DO: duplicate state, revive retired architecture, or claim integration from compile success alone.

### 35. Phase 2 data and persistence

Phase 2 adds one validator-backed sample for oral histories, teaching circles, witness disagreements, welcome rituals, archive callbacks, and civic repair, then proves migration or stateless reconstruction. Every field is classified as authored, derived, or durable. Save capture and restore are written before any claim of persistence. The phase must not widen the catalog or rename shared IDs until the sample is reachable and replay-stable.


### 36. Phase 3 host and route

Phase 3 binds the Core result to a thin host route. It preserves route registration, focus, close/back, refresh, disposal, error, stale, and empty behavior. The phase completes when a player can perform one lesson record and callback with consent, redaction, accessibility, unresolved evidence, and later journal projection and see an observable owner result. It must not place domain calculations in the panel or create a local cache.


### 37. Phase 4 content and callbacks

Phase 4 adds one location or room, one named person or crew, one disturbance, and four outcome branches over the proven route. It connects to journal, archive, relationship, roster, route, shelter, or expedition facts only through current owners. It must not enable a content bank that has no runtime consumer.


### 38. File impact map

Likely areas are Assets/Ashfall.Core for owner contracts, Assets/StreamingAssets/Data for the representative row, src for provider and panel binding, and Ashfall.Core.Tests for focused contracts. Each path remains READ ONLY during premise audit and becomes MODIFY only after an exact claim is accepted. Generated outputs are changed only through their owning generator. No Unity-era path is a target.


### 39. Out of scope

Out of scope for Learning Houses, Oral History, and Civic Teaching: a new global simulation, a second ledger, broad architecture cleanup, unrequested localization sweep, unrelated balance changes, retired Unity behavior, speculative save sections, private telemetry, and a global content browser. Nearby gaps remain visible in the blocker receipt and are not silently bundled.


### 40. Rollback and recovery

Rollback for Learning Houses, Oral History, and Civic Teaching removes or disables the new adapter and content row while preserving old saves. A migration failure rejects the malformed envelope without partial mutation. A host failure unbinds the route while Core remains isolated. A content correction appends evidence. The smallest reversible package is one owner seam, one data row, one host route, one focused test set, and one handoff.


### 41. Definition of done

The slice is ready for implementation handoff when owner, consumer, data, state, save or stateless proof, deterministic replay, host route, accessibility, performance, content callback, focused verification, rollback, and exact file claims all have receipts. The package remains READY FOR PREMISE AUDIT until current evidence confirms the first gate. Word count and prose density never promote it.


### 42. Implementation handoff

The handoff for Learning Houses, Oral History, and Civic Teaching names the objective, current reality, required delta, owner, data row, command, route, save impact, replay fixture, accessibility case, performance budget, content IDs, rollback, open blockers, and next bounded action. MUST PRESERVE: one owner, engine-free Core, JSON authority, deterministic behavior, current save ownership, truthful UI, and current ledger claims. MUST NOT DO: duplicate state, revive retired architecture, or claim integration from compile success alone.
