## 43. Cross-plan ownership matrix

| Concern | Sole authority | Plans that read it | Plans allowed to request mutation | Closeout rule |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| inventory and item custody | current inventory owner | 1,2,3,5,7,9,11 | owner only | panels and narrative never decrement locally |
| health and disease | current health/disease owner | 1,2,6,9 | health owner only | outreach can refer or transport without implying treatment |
| route and expedition | current route/expedition owner | 1,2,5,8,10 | route owner only | arrival projections carry source age and delay |
| weather and hazards | current weather/disaster owners | 1,5,7,8,10 | hazard owner only | no plan creates a second weather clock |
| water and treatment | current water/fluid/treatment owners | 3,7,9 | owner only | public works records requests, not duplicate flow |
| power, warmth, air | current shelter infrastructure owners | 1,3,7,9,10 | owner only | automation displays reserve facts and existing commands |
| relationships and consent | current relationship/consent owners | 2,4,6,12 | owner only | private facts are redacted or withheld by owner |
| journal and archive | current journal/archive owners | 1,2,3,4,6,8,9,10,11,12 | owner only | projections cite source IDs and revision state |
| roster and duty | current roster/duty owners | 1,2,3,6,7,10,11,12 | owner only | no plan allocates a worker in a panel |
| cartography and provenance | current map/archive owners | 1,4,5,8,10,12 | owner only | corrections append evidence and preserve prior marks |
| scenario and campaign | current narrative/campaign owners | 1,2,3,4,6,9,10,12 | owner only | package lifecycle is explicit and bounded |
| accessibility and presentation | UserSettings and existing UI authorities | all plans | presentation owner only | no feature-specific preference ledger |

## 44. Cross-plan event order

The program uses a common event order to prevent hidden cycles. First, an upstream authority publishes a fact with source ID, source age, and event sequence. Second, a canonical owner evaluates a command or query and emits a typed result. Third, projections such as journal, archive, roster, relationship, or map surfaces consume the result. Fourth, narrative content schedules a delayed callback using a stable content ID and lifecycle. Fifth, the host refreshes visible state and reports feedback. A projection may request another owner mutation only through that owner's command; it may not mutate a foreign ledger.

The preferred order for hazard and travel is weather or world fact → expedition or route result → cargo or shelter consequence → journal/archive callback → UI. The preferred order for medical outreach is health or consent fact → transport or referral result → inventory custody → journal/relationship callback → UI. The preferred order for public works and automation is water, power, building, duty, or project fact → owner result → reputation or archive projection → UI. The preferred order for memory and learning is relationship or consent fact → journal/archive result → scenario or roster callback → UI.

## 45. Pairwise bridge register

Each bridge below is a review packet, not a claim that the pair is already wired.


### Bridge Plan 1 ↔ Plan 5

The bridge exists to coordinate burned routes and weather delays affect supply and return timing. The premise receipt must name the upstream publisher, downstream consumer, freshness rule, and delayed callback. The first slice reads one fact, presents uncertainty, offers verify/defer/refuse/return actions, and preserves the prior state on refusal. It does not copy mutable values into the partner plan. Save behavior is either a rebuildable projection or an additive owner field with CaptureState and RestoreState. The same-seed fixture repeats the pair through save, reload, duplicate delivery, interruption, and content correction. The bridge stops if either side lacks a current route, if event order is ambiguous, or if a panel would need to calculate the partner result.

## 46. Dependency waves and package order

### Wave A — premise and ownership

Audit Plans 1–4 first because they define hazard, care, civic, and scenario boundaries. In parallel, audit one read-only seam from Plans 5–12. Release no new data rows until the owner matrix and collision list are current.

### Wave B — owner contracts

Implement only the smallest Core query or command for one plan in each band: hazard observation, consent-aware referral, service request, scenario package, arrival proof, memory projection, reserve projection, survey provenance, preservation batch, perimeter signal, tool checkout, and lesson record. Each package remains isolated and receives focused tests before any cross-plan listener is added.

### Wave C — persistence and replay

Pair the twelve first slices through save/reload or explicit stateless proof. Add migration defaults only where durable state is justified. Record event order, duplicate handling, correction behavior, and state hashes.

### Wave D — host routes and feedback

Bind one route per first slice. Verify focus, keyboard/controller parity, empty/stale/error states, close/back behavior, long text, reduced motion, sound-off alternatives, and disposal.

### Wave E — representative content

Add one sample location or room, one named person or crew, one disturbance, four outcomes, one delayed callback, and one retirement rule per plan. Do not expand banks until the representative content can reach a real owner result.

### Wave F — paired bridges

Enable only those pairwise bridges whose upstream and downstream receipts pass. The integrator verifies event order and rollback after each bridge, then updates the live ledger if implementation is authorized.

### Wave G — bounded scale and closeout

Increase content volume, pagination, and performance only after correctness. Closeout requires a receipt per plan and a program matrix showing owner, consumer, persistence, replay, UI, content, performance, rollback, and next action.

## 47. Program-wide failure matrix

| Failure | Expected response | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| owner absent | stop and mark UNOWNED | source search and decision request |
| consumer absent | keep data design-only | route search and reachability note |
| duplicate ID | reject row with per-row diagnostic | validator output |
| missing reference | reject affected row, preserve neighbors | reference report |
| stale source | display age and next action | UI receipt |
| duplicate event | fold or reject at owner boundary | event-order test |
| interrupted save | restore one coherent state | round-trip fixture |
| corrected content | append revision, preserve old evidence | migration fixture |
| zero resources | refuse with explanation, no negative state | boundary test |
| dead actor or unavailable route | expose blocked/expired outcome | state transition test |
| host not mounted | no mutation; safe no-op or queued refresh | lifecycle test |
| performance over budget | degrade presentation, preserve authority | bounded profile |
| accessibility failure | stop rollout until alternate route works | a11y receipt |
| rollback failure | block next package | rollback fixture |

## 48. File impact map and ownership claims

| Area | Default action | Reason | Risk control |
|---|---|---|---|
| Assets/Ashfall.Core | READ first; MODIFY only for proven owner seam | reusable domain behavior and state | engine-free contract and focused tests |
| Assets/StreamingAssets/Data | READ first; ADD one validated row at a time | authoritative authored content | schema, reference, and reachability gates |
| src | READ first; MODIFY only for thin route | input, binding, presentation, lifecycle | no gameplay logic in panels |
| Ashfall.Core.Tests | ADD focused contract tests after premise proof | invariants, save, replay, data, event checks | test file alone before aggregate |
| docs/plans/expansion_wave1 | MODIFY for receipts and closeout | planning and handoff authority | documentation-only status |
| INTEGRATION_PLANS.md | MODIFY only by foreman/integrator | live queue and acceptance | no builder edits |
| WORKTREE_OWNERSHIP.md | MODIFY only for claims | path ownership | exact files and release notes |
| generated manifests | MODIFY through generator | avoid manual drift | owning generator --check |
| Unity-era Assets/_Game | READ ONLY reference | retired migration input | never add new gameplay |

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

## 50. Rollback program and recovery playbook

Rollback is package-scoped. Disable the host route first, preserve the old save envelope, and leave the Core seam unused if the presenter is not safe. If data is the problem, mark the row disabled or corrected through its lifecycle rather than deleting evidence. If a migration is the problem, reject the malformed envelope before applying partial state. If a cross-plan bridge is the problem, remove the listener or adapter and return each plan to its prior owner-only behavior. If a narrative callback is the problem, retire the content ID while preserving journal or archive evidence already emitted by the owner. A rollback receipt records the trigger, affected files, player-visible result, old-save behavior, and next premise audit.

## 51. Definition of program closeout

The Plans 1–12 program reaches documentation closeout when the central file, twelve plan receipts, README synchronization, and existing closeout packets agree on the following facts: every proposal has one owner family; every first slice has a named consumer; every durable claim has save and migration rules; every replayable result has deterministic ordering; every host route has accessibility and lifecycle behavior; every content packet has a reachable fact and delayed callback; every performance claim has a bounded fixture; every implementation package has a rollback; and every blocker is visible.

This closeout does not authorize implementation. Authorization remains with the current foreman, queue, path claims, and user direction. The next action is one premise audit selected from the matrix, followed by a small owned change and focused verification. The program remains healthy when it stops at missing authority rather than inventing a substitute.

## 52. Implementation handoff contract

### MUST PRESERVE

One authority per concern; engine-free Core; JSON data authority; deterministic seeded behavior; current save ownership; truthful UI projection; keyboard/controller parity; accessibility; current queue; current path claims; and visible blockers.

### MUST ADD

Premise receipts; owner-bound Core seams; validator-backed representative rows; capture/restore or stateless proof; paired replay fixtures; thin host routes; one accessible content slice; bounded performance evidence; rollback; and an explicit handoff.

### MUST NOT DO

Do not create a parallel resource, ledger, route, relationship graph, archive, campaign manager, alert bus, condition store, or lesson database. Do not add Unity gameplay logic. Do not convert prose into runtime claims. Do not revive retired APIs. Do not edit claimed paths. Do not call a catalog row integrated until a current route reaches it.

### VERIFY WITH

Focused Core tests; save round-trip and migration tests; deterministic replay; catalog and reference validation; Core-to-provider route checks; bounded Godot headless checks where necessary; accessibility cases; performance budget; and rollback fixture.

### FIRST SAFE IMPLEMENTATION STEP

Select one plan from the matrix, inspect current source and data, write the premise receipt, claim exact paths, and implement only the smallest owner-bound read model or command. Stop if the current owner, consumer, or save seam cannot be proven.

The artifact closes only when rollback can be executed without deleting old evidence or making old saves unreadable.

## 54. Multi-plan closeout rehearsal set

The integrator rehearses the full program with one bounded sample from each plan. The rehearsal begins with Plans 1 and 10 publishing hazard or perimeter observations, passes through Plans 5 and 8 for route and provenance, and then crosses Plans 7 and 9 for shelter and kitchen consequences. Plans 2 and 6 test consent, privacy, and relationship callbacks. Plans 3 and 11 test public works, condition, and repair custody. Plans 4 and 12 test scenario packaging, oral history, and content correction. The rehearsal must prove that every projection can be rebuilt, every command reaches one owner, every duplicate event is folded, and every UI route remains accessible when an optional data or audio asset is absent.

The rehearsal includes a cold start, a loaded old save, an interrupted command, a duplicated event, a corrected content row, a redacted record, an unavailable location, a zero-resource refusal, a dead actor, a very large record page, and a controller-only pass. It records source age, event order, state hash, save checksum, route focus, feedback text, performance timing, and rollback result. A failure in one plan does not authorize a local workaround in another plan; the bridge is marked blocked and returned to the owner.
